<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```

<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 22106)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"3f3afb","wall_time_seconds":0.9687662,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":21511,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT teaching standard, selected 2026-10-02:** Joshua chose the supplied Conditional Reasoning transcription as the reference for how LSAT material should sound and unfold. Read [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) before drafting lessons, notes, explanations, drills, flashcards, quizzes, or instructional feedback. It contains the detailed source analysis, original calibration examples, progression, and review rubric. [Source identity and limits](sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) are preserved separately. This choice develops the teaching-content register below; correspondence and marketing retain their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Air between ideas. Real correspondence is short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters). In teaching, vary sentence and paragraph length with the reasoning: give an explanation room, then let a short sentence settle the consequence. Do not impose email brevity on a lesson or cut its intermediate reasoning.\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\"; \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs: never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n> Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Apology first, as the opening line.** \\"I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\" The ownership sits in the first two sentences, before any explanation.\\r\\n2. **State their position plainly, as a confirmation.** \\"To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet.\\" Answer the question they actually asked. Don\'t mention the package or credits unless the situation calls for it.\\r\\n3. **One-sentence explanation, then \\"That is fixed now.\\"** No mechanics, no system names.\\r\\n4. **Hand them the next step, plus the manual fallback.** The link, \\"pick any time it shows and it will book,\\" and the offer to do it by hand: \\"If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\"\\r\\n5. **Invite the next report:** \\"If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\"\\r\\n6. **Close with \\"Once again, my apologies.\\"** then \\"Best,\\" and \\"Joshua\\".\\r\\n\\r\\nCut from the draft, do not reuse:\\r\\n- \\"Sorry for the runaround\\" as a sign-off. The apology opens the email; it is not a closing quip.\\r\\n- \\"You will get a calendar invite with the Google Meet link right after.\\" Do not narrate what the system will send; it reads as a promise about machinery.\\r\\n- \\"You have all 10 sessions\\" / \\"nothing was used from your package.\\" Leave the package out unless it is needed; the reassurance they want is that nothing is booked, not an accounting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Situational micro-templates\\r\\n\\r\\nVerbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.\\r\\n  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don\'t worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.\\r\\n- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua\'s explicit go-ahead.\\r\\n- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**\\r\\n  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.\\r\\n- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.\\r\\n  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I\'m glad we got to talk through where you\'re at and what you\'re aiming for on test day.\\r\\n- **Inbound prospect (hasn\'t booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.\\r\\n  > Thanks for reaching out ... I\'d be happy to talk more about what you\'re working on and see if we\'d be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.\\r\\n- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.\\r\\n  > Please don\'t apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The three registers\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich voice governs which surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, personal encouragement, email\\r\\n\\r\\nFully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.\\r\\n\\r\\nReference line (target tone):\\r\\n\\r\\n> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let\'s slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You\'ve got this.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it\'s genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.\\r\\n  > Missing this is expected. It\'s one of the hardest moves on the test. Let\'s slow down and see where it slipped.\\r\\n- **Accountability (student isn\'t doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never \\"the reps aren\'t happening,\\" never tough love.\\r\\n- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.\\r\\n  > That\'s the cleanest you\'ve split premise from conclusion yet. Let\'s push it into the harder sets.\\r\\n- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. \\"Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Teaching content (conversational reasoning) -- lessons, recaps, notes, drills, explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nUse [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md), Joshua\'s selected calibration from 2026-10-02. Keep the reader beside the teacher. Explain the ordinary meaning, ground it in an original example, make the small inferences audible, ask a useful question, and show both the consequence and its limit. Introduce terminology and notation when their meaning is established. Give each concept enough development to be learned from the page.\\r\\n\\r\\nContractions and varied sentence lengths should sound natural when read aloud. Repetition should change the reader\'s view of the same relationship. Questions should give the reader actual thinking work. Light humor may appear within an explanation when it helps understanding; this supersedes the older wit-only-at-the-edges restriction. Do not force punchlines, slang, hyperbole, catchphrases, or paragraph quotas.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source governs delivery, not Joshua\'s procedure, indicator taxonomy, biography, or the scope of a lesson. Preserve his approved methods and logical precision. Personal encouragement still follows the earnest coaching register. Project-owned `lsat-u-voice` routes here and to the detailed standard; an older vendor writer-blend skill must not override this selection.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing\\r\\n\\r\\nConfident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed \\"the next card is the page in front of you\\" was clever but empty).\\r\\n\\r\\n> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.\\r\\n\\r\\nBrand copy speaks as the platform (\\"we\\", \\"you\\"), not as an individual (\\"I\\", \\"Joshua\\"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Don\'t\\r\\n\\r\\n- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)\\r\\n- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.\\r\\n- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging (\\"you might want to\\").\\r\\n- No hype/startup vocabulary.\\r\\n- No em dashes, ever.\\r\\n\\r\\nCorrections log: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]] records every student-facing wording fix and\\r\\nthe reason it was wrong. Read it before writing or editing LSAT U copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).\\r\\n---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: LSAT teaching voice standard\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-03\\r\\naliases: [LSAT voice reference, Conditional transcription writing standard]\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, writing]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-carry-into-the-conditional-bootcamp)\\r\\n- [Accuracy and method boundaries](#accuracy-and-method-boundaries)\\r\\n- [Calibration examples and review](#calibration-examples-for-different-lsat-surfaces)\\r\\n- [A reusable writing brief](#a-reusable-writing-brief)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The evidence and coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. Every supplied section, FAQ entry, table, worked diagram, and drill item was read. HTML line references below refer to that unchanged archived file.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe document has six main sections and six subordinate headings. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML says its formatting was adjusted. We can examine what a pause, table, side note, or separated example does for learning. We cannot claim its font, spacing, color, or CSS exactly reproduces the original publication. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator\'s teaching voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The voice in one original passage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.\\r\\n\\r\\n> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn\'t been inspected would break the rule.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can\'t see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> We don\'t know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn\'t tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> That\'s the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the opening definition before the example, the small prediction, the explanation connecting the rule to the case, and the change in what the reader is given. The last sentence names a distinction the reader has just seen. The passage has a conversational rhythm because it follows a person\'s thoughts. Casual vocabulary alone would not accomplish that.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn an interactive lesson, pause after the first question and again after the inspection-stamp question. Reveal each explanation after the learner has had an opportunity to answer. The printed passage shows the whole progression for calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the teacher relates to the reader\\r\\n\\r\\n### The reader is beside the teacher\\r\\n\\r\\nThe narrator talks directly to someone doing the work. “Let\'s,” “you,” and “we” keep the reader involved. The writing seldom steps back to describe what students generally do. It asks this reader to notice, try, decide, or check something now.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe reader is treated as capable of understanding ordinary meaning. The teacher supplies support when a technical step is new. In the opening, the source first gives the literal relationship, then asks the reader to take its certainty seriously. Later, it encourages a guess before revealing the contrapositive. These are expectations of participation, not claims that everything should feel easy…12106 tokens truncated…n| **Vocabulary** | Keeps useful technical terms, explains them, and avoids internal jargon. |\\r\\n| **Questions and pauses** | Asks a genuine decision and gives an appropriate chance to answer. |\\r\\n| **Boundaries** | Shows what the information leaves open and why a tempting move fails. |\\r\\n| **Progression** | Each operation has a prerequisite, a reason to appear, and adequate practice. |\\r\\n| **Transfer** | Fresh questions require the taught skill, with reasoned feedback. |\\r\\n| **Tone and warmth** | Expects effort, supports the next action, and never mocks uncertainty. |\\r\\n| **Presentation** | Examples, comparisons, notation, and interactions clarify the reasoning. |\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not treat a high total as proof of an identical voice. Use the original source and Joshua\'s response to calibrate the subjective feel. His concrete corrections override a score.\\r\\n\\r\\n## A reusable writing brief\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this when giving another writer or agent the task. Supply the actual source, lesson scope, prerequisite knowledge, and approved method along with it.\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nWrite this LSAT material in Joshua\'s selected teaching voice.\\r\\nRead wiki/teaching-voice.md, references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md,\\r\\nand wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md before drafting.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the reader beside the teacher. Start with the ordinary meaning of\\r\\nthe idea, then make it concrete with an original, intuitive example.\\r\\nShow each small inference in natural language. Ask the question a learner\\r\\nwould actually need to answer here. When the question is practice, leave\\r\\nroom for an attempt before revealing the reasoning.\\r\\n\\r\\nVary sentence length by purpose. Let explanations breathe and use short\\r\\nsentences to settle a consequence or a limit. Use contractions naturally.\\r\\nTeach the technical term when there is a clear idea to attach it to.\\r\\nRepeat an anchor only when the repetition changes the learner\'s view.\\r\\nShow a tempting mistake and how to detect it when that distinction matters.\\r\\n\\r\\nPreserve Joshua\'s method, the exact logical strength, complete conditions,\\r\\nand the selected topic scope. Give fresh practice with explained answers.\\r\\nDo not copy the reference\'s examples, jokes, personal claims, or procedural\\r\\ncounts. No em dashes, hype, invented evidence, internal stage language,\\r\\nor decorative catchphrases. Light humor is optional and must help the lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead the result aloud, check every inference, and review the student-facing\\r\\nprompt and answer states. If the reader cannot hear why the next thought\\r\\nfollows, revise the explanation before polishing the surface.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Keep this reference current\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file is the detailed method reference, not a second canonical brand voice. [Teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md) remains the router. [COPY-CORRECTIONS](../wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md) remains the record of demonstrated wording failures. Add Joshua\'s later calibration to the relevant section here rather than creating competing LSAT voice documents.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe project-owned `lsat-u-voice` skill reads this standard. Recap and bootcamp workflows also read it explicitly. An older vendor voice skill describes a different writer blend; it must not override this Joshua-selected standard in this project. Managed plugin caches are preserved rather than silently rewritten.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source archive is immutable. Future evidence or revised attachments should be recorded as a separate generation with provenance, not written over the original.\\r\\n# Copy corrections\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before\\r\\nwriting or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompanion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This\\r\\nsays what has actually gone wrong and why.\\r\\n\\r\\nStarted 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a\\r\\ncorrection is made.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The rules these corrections came from\\r\\n\\r\\nThree rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was\\r\\nenforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface\\r\\nadded later starts uncovered.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.\\r\\n- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`\\r\\n  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.\\r\\n- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nGuards now live with the content rather than with each component:\\r\\n`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every\\r\\nsurface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Corrections\\r\\n\\r\\n### Conditional opening: define the words before using them (2026-10-03)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s marked browser paragraphs used “direction” and “guarantee” before making their meaning clear. The purpose paragraph also described an argument accepting more than its information supports without showing what that meant. Teach what a rule lets you conclude in plain language, then attach the vocabulary to a concrete relationship. Explain sufficient as enough and necessary as required before relying on those roles. Show what the must-arrow records and why English clause order does not determine the two sides.\\r\\n\\r\\n2026-10-03 clarification: explicitly distinguish the indicator from the complete condition it refers to. Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right. The indicator itself is not a condition in the diagram. Show the same relationship with if at the beginning and in the middle, and show necessary targets both before and after requirement language. Neither sentence position nor the words immediately following the indicator determine placement. Preserve if versus only if as different complete expressions.\\r\\n\\r\\nA rule describes what would follow **if** a condition were true; it does not say that condition happened. Bold that “if” and use meaningful in-view motion to expose the connection, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. Replace compressed ready-order wording with a natural full sentence. Give each paragraph and interaction a specific teaching job. These corrections supplement the canonical voice standard rather than adding a new writing register.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Generic reasoning prompts → concrete numbered demonstrations (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: \\"Read each whole idea,\\" \\"Find what the statements guarantee,\\" the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show two matching rules and their new conditional conclusion. Labels should identify actual inputs or outputs. Review the whole note for repeated umbrella instructions and make task instructions executable. This follows the teaching voice: specific, concrete, short sentences, with air between distinct ideas.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Fact and rule definitions must explain the inference (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Define the inputs with examples, then show the matching step and exact output. A case fact plus a rule proves something about that case. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a separate step. Explain all four fact situations and a hidden chain revealed by contraposition rather than leaving them implicit in an interactive model. Rules include general logical relationships, not only policies or obligations.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Boxed `L -> R` → bold teaching notation **L → R** (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected the recap\'s small monospace arrow inside a pale code box. It looked like implementation text and weakened the explanation. Use a real, clearly weighted arrow in ordinary teaching typography. Explain its complete conditions before shortening them. The Some, Most, and All reference also governs the progression: introduce the whole idea, develop the individual components, then compare and combine them. A visual refresh must review that sequence, not only colors and boxes.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Concept lesson\\" → the actual reasoning title (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe recap preview exposed an internal label above the student lesson. Joshua explicitly rejected it. Use a title such as \\"Facts, Rules, and What Follows\\" and headings that teach the reasoning. Keep production labels, version metadata, and design commentary in private records. Check the rendered HTML, since its shell can introduce wording absent from the Markdown source.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Recall Rephrase Colour\\" → \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\"\\r\\nThe door\'s heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line\\r\\nunder a chip reading \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\". \\"Rephrase\\" belongs to a different\\r\\nActivity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why\\r\\n2,600 passing tests never caught it.\\r\\n**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that\\r\\nit matches.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…\\" → plain instruction\\r\\nClinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read \\"with full\\r\\nintonation\\" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation\\r\\ninternal, \\"hear it naturally in your head\\", had been dropped in an earlier edit.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a word like \\"intake\\" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,\\r\\ncheck what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Three beats through the stimulus\\" → \\"Three steps\\"\\r\\n\\"Beat\\" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary\\r\\nsays students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four\\r\\nthings Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.\\r\\n**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"22 pieces to read\\" → line removed\\r\\nA **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same\\r\\npage use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so \\"22 pieces\\" read as 22\\r\\nsentences.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word\\r\\nalready means on the page before reusing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"say it back\\" → \\"tell it back\\", \\"The Read\\" → \\"The Recall\\"\\r\\nThe library door still carried both retired names. \\"The Read\\" was step four\'s\\r\\nformer name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was\\r\\nstale for months because it is a different database row from the sections.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"then a fourth\\" → \\"then a fourth step\\"\\r\\nThe noun sat three words back and the reader had to carry it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"once you have got all of it\\" → \\"once you have read all of it\\"\\r\\nAmbiguous between having read it and having remembered it, in the one clause\\r\\nthat triggers step four.\\r\\n**Lesson:** precision matters most where the sentence is an instruction.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The three-fact notice → one fact\\r\\n\\"Nothing is recording yet, the microphone opens on Start, and the section\\r\\nrecords in one take that you cannot pause.\\" Two of those are said again in step\\r\\nfour and in the long version, so she skipped the paragraph.\\r\\n**Lesson:** say it once, in the place it matters.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The method, told twice → the long version collapsed\\r\\nThe page rendered four numbered steps and then the whole written briefing saying\\r\\nthe same thing at length, both open. She read the method twice and could not\\r\\ntell which one to follow.\\r\\n**Lesson:** two explanations of one thing is worse than either alone.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"PT105.S1 · 24 stimuli · about 25 min\\" → removed\\r\\nThe section was already the second line of the title. The clock is at the foot\\r\\nand in the long version. The stimulus count measured the work in a unit she has\\r\\nno feel for.\\r\\n**Lesson:** delete a line that repeats what is above it. Do not reword it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** \\"70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there\'s no rush to use them.\\"\\r\\n**Keep:** \\"You\'re set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nSession length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking\\r\\npage when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask\\r\\nfor and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because\\r\\nit tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it\'s for, how many sessions, and the link.\\r\\nStop there.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Drill hub: say what the button does, not what the feature is called (2026-09-09)\\r\\n\\r\\nSix changes on `/classroom/drill`, from the run-3 pass. The hub named its own\\r\\nfeatures, and it assumed the student had rejected something.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Was | Now |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| \\"Start Smart Drill\\" | \\"Practice your weakest types\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Start One Passage\\" | \\"Read one passage\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Start a Smart Drill instead\\" | \\"Or practice your weakest types\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Read one passage instead\\" | \\"Or read one passage now\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Open analytics\\" | \\"See where you lose points\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Open journal\\" | \\"Review your missed questions\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Smart Drill\\" is the product\'s internal name for the adaptive set. On a button it\\r\\nasks the student to guess what she is about to get. The sentence above the button\\r\\nstill names it, which is where a product name belongs: after the thing has been\\r\\nexplained, not as the explanation.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Instead\\" was the worse one. It tells the student she has just declined the\\r\\nassigned work by reading past it. She has not. \\"Or\\" offers a second path without\\r\\nnarrating a decision she never made.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Open analytics\\" and \\"Open journal\\" name a screen. A student does not want\\r\\nanalytics, she wants to know where the points are going.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fallback paragraph lost \\"an adaptive set that follows your weakest question\\r\\ntypes\\" for \\"the question types you miss most\\": the same fact, without the product\\r\\nvocabulary.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** a button says what happens next, in the student\'s words. Feature names\\r\\nbelong in the sentence that explains them, never on the control.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Words never to use in student copy\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Never | Use instead |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| beat, beats | step |\\r\\n| chunk | piece |\\r\\n| intake | plain instruction |\\r\\n| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing\'s own name |\\r\\n| say it back | tell it back |\\r\\n| The Read | The Recall |\\r\\n| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |\\r\\n| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conditional Rephrase: name the student action, not the internal model (2026-09-15)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “record the route,” “choose the route,” and a map presented as five rules.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** diagram the statement on paper, choose the matching diagram, and organize\\r\\nthe map into four groups. “Required means right” is a separate takeaway because it\\r\\nexplains placement; it is not a fifth group.\\r\\n\\r\\n“Route” described the system’s representation, not the student’s work. Calling five\\r\\nunlike ideas rules also flattened the teaching structure and made the layout harder to\\r\\nread.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** name the action the student can see herself doing. Do not force a teaching\\r\\nreminder into the same hierarchy as the categories it explains.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session recaps: write a reusable reference, not a play-by-play (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** an opener saying the student got two questions right, followed by a summary of\\r\\nwhich answer they chose and how they explained it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** the concept the student needs tomorrow, a visual model of the reasoning, a\\r\\nshort diagnostic check, and the exact practice they need to complete.\\r\\n\\r\\nA student will not remember an isolated answer-choice exchange a day later, and the\\r\\nexchange is not useful unless it demonstrates a reusable rule. A recap should read like\\r\\na compact study note, not a record of the conversation. Name a question only when the\\r\\ndetails make the concept easier to apply. Praise belongs only when it identifies a\\r\\ndurable skill or meaningful change, not merely that an answer was correct.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** organize the recap around what the student can use next. Lead with the\\r\\nconcept, show the logic clearly, and finish with tangible practice.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A rule is not a fact (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** describing a conditional Must Be True lesson as “applying a rule to a fact”\\r\\nwithout checking whether the stimulus supplied a fact at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a fact is an asserted true case or condition. A rule is a general conditional\\r\\nrelationship. It tells you what follows if its trigger is present, but it does not say\\r\\nthat the trigger occurred. Some stimuli contain rules only, so the work is to chain\\r\\nthem. When a fact is present, apply a matching rule or a valid contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** label the role of each sentence before explaining the inference. Never\\r\\ninvent a fact to make a rule fire.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A post-session note is lecture material, not an email (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** a greeting, a dated recap heading, references to a question the student is\\r\\nexpected to remember, and a sign-off.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a concept title, a clear opening distinction, numbered procedures, spaced\\r\\nexamples, and practice that uses the lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** the note should remain useful when the session itself is no longer fresh.\\r\\nIt must teach from the page instead of relying on remembered context.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe concept should not arrive as an abrupt list. Begin with a short explanation of\\r\\nwhy the distinction matters, show the full method at a glance, and then teach each\\r\\npath in the same order. Use tables for comparisons and numbered headings for a\\r\\nprocedure so the student can see the structure before reading the detail.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Define the concept in Joshua\'s language (2026-09-28)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “Some, most, and all tell you how much of a group fits a description.”\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** “Some means at least one. Most means more than half. All means every one,”\\r\\ngrounded in a concrete set of ten cookies before drawing conclusions from the words.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe cut line is vague and does not sound like Joshua teaching. It makes the reader\\r\\ndecode “fits a description” before learning any of the three words. It also risks\\r\\nblurring the difference between a count that is possible and one that is guaranteed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** open with the actual distinction or definition Joshua taught. Use a\\r\\nconcrete example to show what each statement proves, then introduce notation and\\r\\nthe inferences. Do not write an abstract umbrella sentence merely to announce the\\r\\ntopic.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference quality does not authorize reference topics (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua requested the Some, Most, and All note as a teaching-flow and visual-quality reference. Quantity examples and exercises do not belong in the separate facts/rules note. Remove that off-topic material; retain the distinction between what a rule says and what a fact establishes. Before future recaps, read the accumulated corrections in the Post Session Recap skill\'s `references/recap-calibration.md`. Preserve source-driven scope while matching the reference\'s teaching quality.\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content 'C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md'","max_output_tokens":17000}));
