A permit doesn\'t tell you what the stall sells.\\",\\r\\n    readback: \'If a stall sells food at the festival, it has a permit.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nconst targetOptions = (item: MixedItem, index: number) => {\\r\\n  const options = [item.target, item.partial, item.other];\\r\\n  return [...options.slice(index % 3), ...options.slice(0, index % 3)];\\r\\n};\\r\\nconst sideOptions = (item: MixedItem, index: number) => index % 2 === 0 ? [item.right, item.left] : [item.left, item.right];\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction MixedItemCheck({ item, index, onChecked }: { item: MixedItem; index: number; onChecked: (checked: boolean) => void }) {\\r\\n  const [target, setTarget] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [left, setLeft] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const targetQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const sideQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const firstChoice = useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);\\r\\n  const change = (update: () => void) => { update(); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); };\\r\\n  const reset = () => { firstChoice.current?.focus(); setTarget(null); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); };\\r\\n  const targetCorrect = target === item.target;\\r\\n  const sideCorrect = left === item.left;\\r\\n  const number = index + 1;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-lab co-check\\" data-mixed-item={number}>\\r\\n    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote>\\r\\n    <h3 id={targetQuestion}>What does <strong>{item.word}</strong> refer to? Choose the complete idea.</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={targetQuestion}>{targetOptions(item, index).map((option, optionIndex) => <button ref={optionIndex === 0 ? firstChoice : undefined} type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={target === option} onClick={() => change(() => setTarget(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <h3 id={sideQuestion}>Which complete idea goes on the left of the must arrow?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={sideQuestion}>{sideOptions(item, index).map(option => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={left === option} onClick={() => change(() => setLeft(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    {left && <RuleDiagram left={left} right={left === item.left ? item.right : item.left} label={`Your diagram: ${left} implies ${left === item.left ? item.right : item.left}`}/>}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cr-control-row\\"><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={target === null || left === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); onChecked(true); }}>Check sentence {number}</button><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>Try sentence {number} again</button></div>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`co-feedback ${targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}>\\r\\n      <strong>{targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? \'Your translation preserves the rule.\' : \'Revise the part described below.\'}</strong>\\r\\n      <p>{targetCorrect ? `You found the complete target: ${item.target}.` : target === item.partial ? item.partialFeedback : item.otherFeedback}</p>\\r\\n      <p>{sideCorrect ? \'Your arrow keeps the direction the sentence gives.\' : item.reversed}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Here, <strong>{item.word}</strong> points to what is {item.family === \'sufficient\' ? \'enough, so its target goes on the left\' : \'required, so its target goes on the right\'}. {item.why}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Read it back: {item.readback}</p>\\r\\n    </div>}</div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function MixedFamilyPractice() {\\r\\n  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState<boolean[]>(() => mixedFamilyItems.map(() => false));\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <p>These sentences mix both kinds of expression, and nothing tells you which kind is coming. For each one, find the complete idea the expression refers to. Then decide which idea is enough and put it on the left. Check your translation before moving on.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\" role=\\"status\\">Sentence {current + 1} of {mixedFamilyItems.length}</p>\\r\\n    {mixedFamilyItems.map((item, index) => <div key={item.sentence} hidden={current !== index}><MixedItemCheck item={item} index={index} onChecked={value => setChecked(previous => previous.map((old, itemIndex) => itemIndex === index ? value : old))}/></div>)}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => setCurrent(current - 1)}>Previous mixed sentence</button><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === mixedFamilyItems.length - 1 || !checked[current]} onClick={() => setCurrent(current + 1)}>Next mixed sentence</button></div>\\r\\n    <p>Moving between sentences keeps your choices and feedback.</p>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

If it makes a promise the original didn\'t make, change the direction.</p>\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:880:        ? [\'You reversed the if relationship. Submission before noon is enough for review; the sentence has not said review proves when the report was submitted.\', \'This preserves the if-clause as the sufficient condition. Submission before noon gives us review even though it appears second in the original sentence.\']\\r\\n"}'}]

A sender who paid could have a parcel without this tag; payment alone does not settle the tag.\', readback: \'If a parcel has a red tracking tag, its sender has paid for tracking.\', contraReadback: \'If its sender has not paid for tracking, the parcel does not have a red tracking tag.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'Every applicant who submits by Friday receives a confirmation.\', left: \'Applicant submits by Friday\', right: \'Applicant receives confirmation\', leftCode: \'SF\', rightCode: \'RC\', originalPosition: 2, contraPosition: 0, why: \'Keep who submits by Friday with applicant. The rule gives those applicants confirmations; it does not restrict all confirmations to them.\', readback: \'If an applicant submits by Friday, the applicant receives a confirmation.\', contraReadback: \'If an applicant does not receive a confirmation, the applicant did not submit by Friday.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'A document is stored in the archive only if it has a catalogue number.\', left: \'Document stored in archive\', right: \'Document has catalogue number\', leftCode: \'A\', rightCode: \'C\', originalPosition: 0, contraPosition: 1, why: \'Only if identifies the catalogue number as required. A numbered document could remain outside the archive.\', readback: \'If a document is stored in the archive, it has a catalogue number.\', contraReadback: \'If a document has no catalogue number, it is not stored in the archive.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'A backup copy is required for a file to be deleted.\', left: \'File is deleted\', right: \'File has backup copy\', leftCode: \'D\', rightCode: \'B\', originalPosition: 1, contraPosition: 0, why: \'The requirement appears first in the sentence. Deletion requires the backup; having a backup does not establish deletion.\', readback: \'If a file is deleted, it has a backup copy.\', contraReadback: \'If a file has no backup copy, it is not deleted under this rule.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'Only guests with prepaid tickets enter the balcony.\', left: \'Guest enters balcony\', right: \'Guest has prepaid ticket\', leftCode: \'E\', rightCode: \'P\', originalPosition: 2, contraPosition: 1, why: \'Only restricts balcony entrants to guests with prepaid tickets. A ticketed guest can stay outside, so the ticket alone is not enough.\', readback: \'If a guest enters the balcony, the guest has a prepaid ticket.\', contraReadback: \'If a guest does not have a prepaid ticket, the guest does not enter the balcony.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'The only recipes printed on the weekly menu are vegan recipes.\', left: \'Recipe printed on weekly menu\', right: \'Recipe is vegan\', leftCode: \'M\', rightCode: \'V\', originalPosition: 0, contraPosition: 2, why: \'The only recipes printed on the weekly menu names the group whose recipes must be vegan. Other vegan recipes can be absent from the menu.\', readback: \'If a recipe is printed on the weekly menu, it is vegan.\', contraReadback: \'If a recipe is not vegan, it is not printed on the weekly menu.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\nfunction practiceChoices(item: PracticeItem, contrapositive: boolean) {\\r\\n  const correct = contrapositive ? `~${item.rightCode} → ~${item.leftCode}` : `${item.leftCode} → ${item.rightCode}`;\\r\\n  const wrong = contrapositive ? [`${item.rightCode} → ${item.leftCode}`, `~${item.leftCode} → ~${item.rightCode}`] : [`${item.rightCode} → ${item.leftCode}`, `~${item.leftCode} → ~${item.rightCode}`];\\r\\n  const position = contrapositive ? item.contraPosition : item.originalPosition;\\r\\n  const options = [...wrong];\\r\\n  options.splice(position, 0, correct);\\r\\n  return options;\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport function IntegratedItem({ item, number, onChecked }: { item: PracticeItem; number: number; onChecked?: (checked: boolean) => void }) {\\r\\n  const [original, setOriginal] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [contra, setContra] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const correct = original === item.originalPosition && contra === item.contraPosition;\\r\\n  const labels = [{ code: item.leftCode, meaning: item.left }, { code: item.rightCode, meaning: item.right }];\\r\\n  if (number % 2 === 0) labels.reverse();\\r\\n  const change = (value: number, isContra: boolean) => { setChecked(false); onChecked?.(false); if (isContra) setContra(value); else setOriginal(value); };\\r\\n  const reset = () => { setOriginal(null); setContra(null); setChecked(false); onChecked?.(false); };\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cr-integrated-item co-check\\" data-reference-practice={number}><header><span className=\\"cr-item-number\\">{String(number).padStart(2, \'0\')}</span><h3>{item.sentence}</h3></header><dl className=\\"cr-practice-legend\\">{labels.map(label => <div key={label.code}><dt>{label.code}</dt><dd>{label.meaning}</dd></div>)}</dl>\\r\\n    <fieldset><legend>Original diagram for item {number}</legend><div className=\\"cr-answer-options cr-symbol-options\\">{practiceChoices(item, false).map((option, index) => <button key={option} type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={original === index} onClick={() => change(index, false)}>{option}</button>)}</div></fieldset>\\r\\n    <fieldset><legend>Contrapositive for item {number}</legend><div className=\\"cr-answer-options cr-symbol-options\\">{practiceChoices(item, true).map((option, index) => <button key={option} type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={contra === index} onClick={() => change(index, true)}>{option}</button>)}</div></fieldset>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cr-control-row\\"><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-primary\\" disabled={original === null || contra === null} aria-label={`Check both diagrams for item ${number}`} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); onChecked?.(true); }}>Check both diagrams</button><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>Try item {number} again</button></div>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`cr-feedback ${correct ? \'cr-correct\' : \'cr-revise\'}`}><strong>{correct ? \'Both diagrams are correct.\' : \'Review the relationship.\'}</strong><p>{original === item.originalPosition ? \'Your original preserves the direction.\' : \'Your original changes the direction or negates conditions the sentence did not negate.\'} {contra === item.contraPosition ? \'Your contrapositive reverses and negates both complete conditions.\' : \'Your contrapositive must start from the missing requirement and rule out the sufficient condition.\'}</p><p>{item.why}</p><p className=\\"cr-answer-key\\">Original: {item.leftCode} → {item.rightCode}<br/>Contrapositive: ~{item.rightCode} → ~{item.leftCode}</p><p>Read the original: {item.readback}</p><p>Read its contrapositive: {item.contraReadback}</p></div>}</div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function conditionalReferenceContent() {\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cr-sequence\\">\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={1} label=\\"Start with meaning\\" title=\\"What does a conditional tell you?\\">\\r\\n      <p>A <strong>condition</strong> is an idea that can be true or false. You win a raffle. You get a bicycle. We can ask whether either condition holds in a particular case.</p>\\r\\n      <p>A <strong>conditional statement</strong> tells us that if one condition is true, another has to be true too. It gives us a rule we can use, even before we know whether either condition is true in a particular case.</p>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"co-if-inline\\">We aren\'t saying the first condition happened. We\'re asking what would follow <strong>if</strong> it did.</p>\\r\\n      <HypotheticalFigure/>\\r\\n      <h3>Why learn this?</h3><p>On the LSAT, you need to distinguish what the information proves from what it leaves open. A conditional rule can help you prove a conclusion. An argument can also misuse the rule and reach a conclusion it hasn\'t proved. We\'ll practice telling those apart.</p>\\r\\n      <p>Let\'s read one rule.</p><blockquote>{raffleRule}</blockquote>\\r\\n      <p>Under this rule, winning the raffle makes getting a bicycle certain. Winning without getting one would break the stated relationship. That is what we mean by a <strong>guarantee</strong> here.</p>\\r\\n      <p>Compare that with “If you win the raffle, you usually get a bicycle.” Usually allows an exception. Our rule does not. Take the stated rule as given; we are asking what it tells us, not whether a real organizer keeps a promise.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Does the rule itself tell us that you actually win the raffle?\\" options={[\'Yes, it says I win.\', \'No, it tells me what follows if I win.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You added a case fact. The rule has not reported a win; it tells you what getting one would establish.\', \'Imagining a win helps us understand the rule. We need a separate fact to establish that you actually win.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>Now let\'s take the two conditions one at a time.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={2} label=\\"Sufficient means enough\\" title=\\"One condition gives you enough\\">\\r\\n      <blockquote>{raffleRule}</blockquote><p>The <strong>if-part</strong> is the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>. Sufficient means enough. Knowing you win is enough, under this rule, to know you get a bicycle.</p>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-role-exhibit\\"><span>Sufficient</span><strong>Win the raffle</strong><p>Enough to establish getting a bicycle</p></div>\\r\\n      <p>You might win. You might not. The rule has not settled that question.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"You don\'t win the raffle. Could you still get a bicycle?\\" options={[\'Yes. Getting a bicycle is still possible.\', \'No. Without winning, getting a bicycle is impossible.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'You could buy one or receive one as a gift. You could also not get one. Neither case creates a winner who fails to get a bicycle, so both fit the rule.\', \'You made winning the only way to get a bicycle. The sentence gives one sufficient condition; it has not excluded other ways.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>Now suppose a fact gives us the sufficient condition. That is when we can <strong>use</strong>, or activate, the rule in its stated direction.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"You win the raffle. Do we need another fact to establish that you get a bicycle?\\" options={[\'Yes, we need another fact about the bicycle.\', \'No, the rule and the win establish it.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'The rule already covers the case in which you win. That fact meets its sufficient condition.\', \'Winning meets the sufficient condition. With the rule, that is enough to establish that you get a bicycle.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>We\'ll now keep this job in mind while changing the wording.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={3} label=\\"The sufficient family\\" title=\\"Different words can preserve the same rule\\">\\r\\n      <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is an expression that helps you recognize a conditional relationship. These expressions can introduce an idea that is enough to establish another.</p>\\r\\n      <p>For the next examples, use a different stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. We will express that same connection in several ways. We haven\'t said everyone with a key borrows a locker.</p>\\r\\n      <WordFamily entries={sufficientFamily} role=\\"sufficient\\"/>\\r\\n      <p>When/whenever and any/anytime group related forms together. An ending changes the kind of case being discussed, but these examples keep the same connection. Read the whole sentence. A familiar word is a clue, not a substitute for its meaning.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Every guest wearing a blue badge receives a lunch voucher. Which complete idea is enough?\\" options={[\'Guest\', \'Guest wearing a blue badge\', \'Receives a lunch voucher\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Guest alone drops the badge restriction and would make a claim about unbadged guests.\', \'The rule gives a voucher to every guest wearing a blue badge. Keep that qualification with the person.\', \'The voucher is what the rule establishes. It has not said every voucher recipient wears a blue badge.\']}/>\\r\\n      <details className=\\"cr-extension\\"><summary>Two more Group 1 expressions</summary><p><strong>Where</strong> can state a relationship across locations: “Where a collection point operates, parcels can be returned.” An operating collection point establishes the availability of returns there. Availability is not proof that someone made a return.</p><p><strong>The only</strong> needs its complete phrase: “The only notebooks on this shelf are hardback.” A notebook on the shelf is hardback. The sentence does not put every hardback notebook there.</p><p>These are part of your Group 1 work. Understand what the full expression covers before using its group as a placement shortcut.</p></details>\\r\\n      <p>We have seen what can be enough. Now let\'s name what that condition requires.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={4} label=\\"Necessary means required\\" title=\\"The other condition has to be there\\">\\r\\n      <blockquote>{raffleRule}</blockquote><p>The <strong>then-part</strong> is the <strong>necessary condition</strong>. Necessary means required. Under this rule, if you win, getting a bicycle has to follow.</p>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-role-exhibit cr-required\\"><span>Necessary</span><strong>Get a bicycle</strong><p>Required whenever you win the raffle</p></div>\\r\\n      <p>Ask “required for what?” Here, a required result of winning under this rule. It is not a requirement you must satisfy before entering. The word necessary describes the relationship, not when the events occur.</p>\\r\\n      <p>We\'re looking at the same rule from its other side. Winning is enough to establish getting a bicycle. Getting a bicycle is the result the rule requires whenever you win.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Under our rule, what has to follow if you win?\\" options={[\'You get a bicycle.\', \'You ride the bicycle home.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Getting a bicycle is the stated result. Winning without getting one would violate the rule.\', \'Riding is not part of this rule. The sentence establishes that you get a bicycle, not what you do with it.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>Necessary does not mean “the next event.” It means required by this relationship. A conditional need not tell you which event caused another, or when either happened.</p>\\r\\n      <p>As with sufficient language, the wording can vary while the relationship stays the same.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={5} label=\\"The necessary family\\" title=\\"Listen for what the sentence needs\\">\\r\\n      <p>Return to our locker rule. Borrowing a locker requires a key. The next expressions all identify that requirement in their complete sentences.</p>\\r\\n      <WordFamily entries={necessaryFamily} role=\\"necessary\\"/>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-contrast\\"><div><h3>If</h3><p>“You borrow a locker <strong>if</strong> you have a key.”</p><p>Having a key is enough for borrowing under this sentence.</p></div><div><h3>Only if</h3><p>“You borrow a locker <strong>only if</strong> you have a key.”</p><p>Having a key is required. This sentence has not made it enough.</p></div></div>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"A guest enters the lounge only if the guest has a current pass. What does entering establish?\\" options={[\'The guest has a current pass.\', \'Every guest with a pass enters.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Only if identifies the current pass as required for entry. An entering guest therefore meets that requirement.\', \'You made the requirement enough. A guest could have a current pass and remain outside without violating the sentence.\']}/>\\r\\n      <details className=\\"cr-extension\\"><summary>More Group 2 forms, with the sentence intact</summary><p><strong>Only when</strong> and <strong>only where</strong> can restrict the occasions or places of an outcome: “The fountain runs only when its pump is on.” A running fountain requires an operating pump. The pump being on does not establish that the fountain runs.</p><p><strong>Requires</strong> directly names a requirement: “Entry requires a current pass.” Preserve current. <strong>Always</strong> needs the sentence\'s subject and scope: “The upstairs room is always quiet when the door is shut.” Here a shut door establishes quiet, not the other way around.</p><p><strong>Only</strong> and <strong>the only</strong> are different complete expressions. “Only people who get a bicycle win this raffle” and “The only people who win this raffle are people who get a bicycle” both preserve the rule: winning establishes getting a bicycle.</p></details>\\r\\n      <p>We can now recognize both roles before writing any symbols. Next, let\'s see what happens when a requirement is missing.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n"}'}]

[item.right, item.left] : [item.left, item.right];\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction MixedItemCheck({ item, index, onChecked }: { item: MixedItem; index: number; onChecked: (checked: boolean) => void }) {\\r\\n  const [draftDiagram, setDraftDiagram] = useState(\'\');\\r\\n  const [draftReadback, setDraftReadback] = useState(\'\');\\r\\n  const [comparing, setComparing] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const draftQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const readbackQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const draftInput = useRef<HTMLTextAreaElement>(null);\\r\\n  const [target, setTarget] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [left, setLeft] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const targetQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const sideQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const change = (update: () => void) => { update(); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); };\\r\\n  const reset = () => { draftInput.current?.focus(); setComparing(false); setTarget(null); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); };\\r\\n  const targetCorrect = target === item.target;\\r\\n  const sideCorrect = left === item.left;\\r\\n  const number = index + 1;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-lab co-check\\" data-mixed-item={number}>\\r\\n    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote>\\r\\n    <p>Write the complete conditions and the arrow yourself. Then read your diagram back as an if/then sentence. You can use words or short labels you define.</p>\\r\\n    <label htmlFor={draftQuestion}>Your diagram for sentence {number}</label>\\r\\n    <textarea ref={draftInput} id={draftQuestion} className=\\"co-attempt-input\\" rows={3} value={draftDiagram} readOnly={comparing} onChange={event => setDraftDiagram(event.target.value)}/>\\r\\n    <label htmlFor={readbackQuestion}>Your English readback for sentence {number}</label>\\r\\n    <textarea id={readbackQuestion} className=\\"co-attempt-input\\" rows={3} value={draftReadback} readOnly={comparing} onChange={event => setDraftReadback(event.target.value)}/>\\r\\n    {!comparing && <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={!draftDiagram.trim() || !draftReadback.trim()} onClick={() => setComparing(true)}>Compare my translation for sentence {number}</button>}\\r\\n    {comparing && <>\\r\\n    <p>Your written answer is saved for you to compare. The choices below check the target and direction; your written answer is not graded automatically.</p>\\r\\n    <h3 id={targetQuestion}>What does <strong>{item.word}</strong> refer to? Choose the complete idea.</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={targetQuestion}>{targetOptions(item, index).map(option => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={target === option} onClick={() => change(() => setTarget(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <h3 id={sideQuestion}>Which complete idea goes on the left of the must arrow?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={sideQuestion}>{sideOptions(item, index).map(option => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={left === option} onClick={() => change(() => setLeft(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    {left && <RuleDiagram left={left} right={left === item.left ? item.right : item.left} label={`Your diagram: ${left} implies ${left === item.left ? item.right : item.left}`}/>}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cr-control-row\\"><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={target === null || left === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); onChecked(true); }}>Check sentence {number}</button><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>Try sentence {number} again</button></div>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`co-feedback ${targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}>\\r\\n      <strong>{targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? \'Your translation preserves the rule.\' : \'Revise the part described below.\'}</strong>\\r\\n      <p>{targetCorrect ? `You found the complete target: ${item.target}.` : target === item.partial ? item.partialFeedback : item.otherFeedback}</p>\\r\\n      <p>{sideCorrect ? \'Your arrow keeps the direction the sentence gives.\' : item.reversed}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Here, <strong>{item.word}</strong> points to what is {item.family === \'sufficient\' ? \'enough, so its target goes on the left\' : \'required, so its target goes on the right\'}. {item.why}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Read it back: {item.readback}</p>\\r\\n    </div>}</div>\\r\\n    </>}\\r\\n    {!comparing && <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>Try sentence {number} again</button>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function MixedFamilyPractice() {\\r\\n  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState<boolean[]>(() => mixedFamilyItems.map(() => false));\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <p>These sentences use both kinds of expression. The family name stays hidden until you check your choices. First translate each sentence yourself, without supplied condition labels. Then compare your work and check the complete target and arrow direction before moving on.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\" role=\\"status\\">Sentence {current + 1} of {mixedFamilyItems.length}</p>\\r\\n    {mixedFamilyItems.map((item, index) => <div key={item.sentence} hidden={current !== index}><MixedItemCheck item={item} index={index} onChecked={value => setChecked(previous => previous.map((old, itemIndex) => itemIndex === index ? value : old))}/></div>)}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => setCurrent(current - 1)}>Previous mixed sentence</button><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === mixedFamilyItems.length - 1 || !checked[current]} onClick={() => setCurrent(current + 1)}>Next mixed sentence</button></div>\\r\\n    <p>Moving between sentences keeps your written attempt, choices, and feedback. Trying a sentence again keeps your writing so you can revise it, and hides the supplied labels until you compare again.</p>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

That supplies an exceptionless rule in this sentence; the gallery sentence reports one occasion.\',\\r\\n        ],\\r\\n      },\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      title: \'Where: keep the location attached\',\\r\\n      paragraphs: [\\r\\n        \\"The sentence tells us about a location where a station is available. At that location, availability is enough for the service to operate. Where refers to the station being available, not to service in some unrelated place.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Keep the location the same on both sides. A short label can save writing, but it still needs to refer to the place the sentence concerns.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      prompt: \'A station is available at North Square. What does the rule establish about North Square?\',\\r\\n      reasoning: [\\r\\n        \\"The service operates there. The fact puts North Square in the locations covered by this rule.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Read the diagram as: if a station is available at a location, the service operates there. This fact doesn\'t give us a conclusion about another square whose station status we don\'t know.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      title: \'All: name the complete group\',\\r\\n      paragraphs: [\\r\\n        \\"The statement covers each registered guest. Once you know someone is a registered guest, you can conclude that person receives a wristband.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Registered matters. The target of all is the complete group registered guests, so the sufficient condition includes registration. The rule doesn\'t tell us about every guest regardless of registration.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      prompt: \\"Nora is a guest, but we don\'t know whether she\'s registered. Does this rule settle her wristband status?\\",\\r\\n      reasoning: [\\r\\n        \\"No. The fact doesn\'t yet put Nora in the complete group the rule covers. She could be registered, but we haven\'t been told that.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Keep registered in your label. Guest → Receives wristband would extend the rule to people the original sentence hasn\'t covered.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      title: \'The only: read the whole expression\',\\r\\n      paragraphs: [\\r\\n        \\"The sentence limits the vehicles permitted inside. Every vehicle in that group has to be electric. Start by asking the only what? The target is vehicles permitted inside, not electric vehicles.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Knowing a vehicle is permitted inside is therefore enough to establish that it is electric. The expression\'s complete target has the sufficient role.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      prompt: \'You learn that a vehicle was permitted inside. What does the sentence tell you about it?\',\\r\\n      reasoning: [\\r\\n        \\"It is electric. The rule covers every permitted vehicle, including this one.\\",\\r\\n        \\"The sentence hasn\'t promised that every electric vehicle is admitted. An electric vehicle that is not permitted inside would fit the rule. Whether a vehicle actually enters is a separate question.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      after: [\\r\\n        \\"This is why the only belongs with our sufficient expressions: the complete group it introduces gives us the starting condition. Read it as a whole expression. We\'ll compare it with only once we\'ve learned the other group.\\",\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      title: \'Every: each qualifying case gets the result\',\\r\\n"}'}]

Put yourself in the proposed starting condition, then check what the original sentence requires you to know. We\'re checking its meaning, not predicting what usually happens to graduates.</p>\\r\\n        <ThinkPause prompt=\\"If I graduate, what must be true of my project?\\">\\r\\n          <p>I\'ve completed it. Now try the reverse below.</p>\\r\\n        </ThinkPause>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n      <ThinkPause prompt={lesson.prompt}>\\r\\n        {lesson.reasoning.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}\\r\\n        <Arrow left={item.left} right={item.right}/>\\r\\n        <p className=\\"co-readback\\">{item.readback}</p>\\r\\n      </ThinkPause>\\r\\n      {lesson.after?.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}\\r\\n      {lesson.check && <Check {...lesson.check}/>}\\r\\n"}'}]

These are possible cases, not a claim that any of them happened.</p>\\r\\n    <table className=\\"co-combination-table\\">\\r\\n      <caption>Possible cases under the raffle rule</caption>\\r\\n      <thead><tr><th scope=\\"col\\">Win the raffle?</th><th scope=\\"col\\">Get a bicycle?</th><th scope=\\"col\\">Fits the rule?</th></tr></thead>\\r\\n      <tbody>\\r\\n        <tr><td>Yes</td><td>Yes</td><td>Yes. The required result is present.</td></tr>\\r\\n        <tr><td>No</td><td>Yes</td><td>Yes. The rule allows a bicycle without a win.</td></tr>\\r\\n        <tr><td>No</td><td>No</td><td>Yes. With no win, the rule settles nothing about a bicycle.</td></tr>\\r\\n        <tr><td>Yes</td><td>No</td><td><strong>No. This is the single violation.</strong></td></tr>\\r\\n      </tbody>\\r\\n    </table>\\r\\n    <p>A conditional is broken when its sufficient condition is present and its necessary condition is absent. That\'s why no bicycle rules out a win: a winner without a bicycle would be the forbidden combination.</p>\\r\\n  </NoteSection>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction MixedPracticeRounds() {\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <NoteSection title=\\"Work through three rounds\\">\\r\\n      <p>First practice the necessary expressions. Necessary-condition practice</a>\\r\\n        <a href=\\"#translation-round\\">2. Mixed translation</a>\\r\\n        <a href=\\"#comparison-round\\">3. Matched against either `metadata_json.bootcamp_id`\\r\\n   * (slug, see `bootcampIdOverride`) or `source_id` on a classroom_items\\r\\n   * row of type bootcamp_module.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  routePath: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Override the slug extracted from `routePath`. New surfaces under /topics/* should pass this\\r\\n   * explicitly (e.g. routePath \\"/topics/abstract\\", bootcampIdOverride\\r\\n   * \\"abstraction\\") so classroom_items rows assigned with the legacy slug\\r\\n   * still resolve to the same gate.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  bootcampIdOverride?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Additional route paths to accept as `source_id` matches. Used when a\\r\\n   * topic has moved (legacy /bootcamp/abstraction → /topics/abstract). The\\r\\n   * current `routePath` is always matched.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  legacyRoutePaths?: string[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * The student-facing display name, used only by the locked state.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  title?: string;\\r\\n  children: ReactNode;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Per-student bootcamp gate. Admins always pass. Students need a\\r\\n * matching `classroom_items` row of type=\'bootcamp_module\' assigned to\\r\\n * them. If no row exists, render a calm \\"Not assigned yet\\" surface.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Direct URL hits go through this gate, so a student typing\\r\\n * /bootcamp/principle-flaw won\'t see anything they weren\'t assigned.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport default function BootcampGate({\\r\\n  routePath,\\r\\n  bootcampIdOverride,\\r\\n  legacyRoutePaths,\\r\\n  title,\\r\\n  children,\\r\\n}: Props) {\\r\\n  const { user, authReady } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const { is_admin, loading: permsLoading } = useUserPermissions();\\r\\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\\r\\n  const bootcampId =\\r\\n    bootcampIdOverride ??\\r\\n    bootcampIdFromRoute(routePath);\\r\\n  const explicitRoutePaths = [routePath, ...(legacyRoutePaths ?? [])];\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: assigned, isLoading } = useQuery<boolean>({\\r\\n    queryKey: [\'bootcamp-gate\', user?.id, routePath, bootcampId],\\r\\n    enabled: !!user && !is_admin && !permsLoading,\\r\\n    queryFn: async () => {\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\\r\\n        .from(\'classroom_items\')\\r\\n        .select(\'id, status, source_id, metadata_json\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'type\', \'bootcamp_module\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'student_id\', user!.id)\\r\\n        .neq(\'status\', \'archived\');\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n      const rows = (data ?? []) as Array<{\\r\\n        source_id: string | null;\\r\\n        metadata_json: Record<string, unknown> | null;\\r\\n      }>;\\r\\n      return rows.some(\\r\\n        (r) =>\\r\\n          classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(r, bootcampId) ||\\r\\n          (r.source_id\\r\\n            ? explicitRoutePaths.some((path) => r.source_id === path || r.source_id?.startsWith(`${path}/`))\\r\\n            : false),\\r\\n      );\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (!authReady || permsLoading) {\\r\\n    return <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-zinc-950\\" />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (is_admin || assigned) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (isLoading) {\\r\\n    return <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-zinc-950\\" />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center px-6 py-12 bg-background\\">\\r\\n      <div className=\\"max-w-md text-center space-y-4\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"mx-auto w-12 h-12 rounded-full bg-muted flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n          <Lock className=\\"w-5 h-5 text-muted-foreground\\" strokeWidth={1.75} />\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"space-y-1.5\\">\\r\\n          <h2 className=\\"text-lg font-semibold\\">Not assigned yet</h2>\\r\\n          <p className=\\"text-sm text-muted-foreground leading-relaxed\\">\\r\\n            {title ? `${title} ` : \'This bootcamp \'}\\r\\n            isn\'t part of your current path. Joshua can add it from the Teacher Workspace.\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n        <Button variant=\\"outline\\" size=\\"sm\\" onClick={() => navigate(\'/classroom\')}>\\r\\n          Back to Classroom\\r\\n        </Button>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { type ReactNode } from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { Navigate, useLocation } from \\"react-router-dom\\";\\r\\nimport { useAuth } from \\"@/contexts/AuthContext\\";\\r\\nimport { useUserPermissions, type PermissionFlag } from \\"@/hooks/useUserPermissions\\";\\r\\nimport { authUrlWithReturnTo } from \\"@/lib/authRedirect\\";\\r\\nimport LockedModule from \\"@/components/LockedModule\\";\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface ProtectedRouteProps {\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Optional feature flag, or a list of acceptable feature flags. Ignored when\\r\\n   * `requireAdmin` is true.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  flag?: PermissionFlag | readonly PermissionFlag[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Allow any signed-in user through even when the feature flag is false.\\r\\n   * Useful for core student workspace pages that should stay reachable while\\r\\n   * their contents are still constrained by row-level data access.\\r\\n   *\\r\\n   * \\"Signed in is enough\\" — never \\"nobody needs to be signed in\\". A route that\\r\\n   * should be public is not wrapped in this component at all.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  allowSignedIn?: boolean;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * When true, only admin users may render the children. Non-admin\\r\\n   * users, regardless of feature flags, see LockedModule.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  requireAdmin?: boolean;\\r\\n  children: ReactNode;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS = new Set([\\"localhost\\", \\"127.0.0.1\\", \\"::1\\"]);\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Opt-in only. A plain `npm run dev` now exercises the real permission path, so\\r\\n * a gating regression is visible locally instead of surfacing after deploy.\\r\\n * Set VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES=1 in .env.local to walk gated routes without the\\r\\n * matching flags.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * It relaxes *flags*, never authentication — the caller applies it only after\\r\\n * the signed-in check, so no configuration can make this component render to an\\r\\n * anonymous visitor.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction isLocalDevAccessAllowed() {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    import.meta.env.DEV &&\\r\\n    import.meta.env.VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES === \\"1\\" &&\\r\\n    LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS.has(window.location.hostname)\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function ProtectedRoute({ flag, requireAdmin, allowSignedIn, children }: ProtectedRouteProps) {\\r\\n  const { user, authReady } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const permissions = useUserPermissions();\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Auth first, and nothing before it. `useUserPermissions` reports\\r\\n  // `loading: authReady && !!user && isPending`, which is *false* while auth is\\r\\n  // still resolving — so a check on `!user` alone read \\"not yet known\\" as\\r\\n  // \\"signed out\\" and bounced every cold deep link to /auth. Holding here until\\r\\n  // auth is definitively resolved is what makes an assignment link survive.\\r\\n  if (!authReady) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Authentication is intrinsic to this component: every shape of it requires a\\r\\n  // user. The check used to run only for `requireAdmin` or flagged routes, so a\\r\\n  // wrapper with `allowSignedIn` and no flag fell through to the unconditional\\r\\n  // render at the bottom and served protected content to anonymous visitors.\\r\\n  if (!user) {\\r\\n    const returnTo = `${location.pathname}${location.search}${location.hash}`;\\r\\n    return <Navigate to={authUrlWithReturnTo(returnTo)} replace />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // After the auth checks, never before: the dev bypass opens locked features\\r\\n  // on a local machine, not the front door.\\r\\n  if (isLocalDevAccessAllowed()) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Only a true cold read blocks; cached reads and background refetches keep\\r\\n  // this false, so gated navigations do not flash.\\r\\n  if (permissions.loading) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (requireAdmin) {\\r\\n    return permissions.is_admin ? <>{children}</> : <LockedModule />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (allowSignedIn) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const flags = Array.isArray(flag) ? flag : flag ? [flag] : [];\\r\\n  if (flags.length > 0) {\\r\\n    if (permissions.is_admin || flags.some((f) => permissions[f])) {\\r\\n      return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    return <LockedModule />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

Everyone lands\\r\\n * on /classroom unless a *validated* target says otherwise.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const DEFAULT_POST_AUTH_TARGET = \\"/classroom\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Where an OAuth departure parks its target, since a ref cannot survive the round trip. */\\r\\nconst STASH_KEY = \\"lsatu_post_auth_target\\";\\r\\nconst STASH_TS_KEY = \\"lsatu_post_auth_target_ts\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * A stashed target older than this is a leftover from an abandoned attempt — the\\r\\n * user opened the tab, wandered off, and came back. params.get(\\"next\\"));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Park a validated target before leaving the page — the OAuth departure leg.\\r\\n * Passing a null/invalid target clears any previous stash rather than keeping a\\r\\n * stale one alive.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function stashReturnTo(raw: string | null | undefined): void {\\r\\n  if (typeof window === \\"undefined\\") return;\\r\\n  const target = parseSafeReturnTo(raw);\\r\\n  if (!target) {\\r\\n    clearStashedReturnTo();\\r\\n    return;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    sessionStorage.setItem(STASH_KEY, target);\\r\\n    sessionStorage.setItem(STASH_TS_KEY, String(Date.now()));\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    // Private mode / storage disabled: the deep link is lost, the sign-in is not.\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function clearStashedReturnTo(): void {\\r\\n  if (typeof window === \\"undefined\\") return;\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    sessionStorage.removeItem(STASH_KEY);\\r\\n    sessionStorage.removeItem(STASH_TS_KEY);\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    /* ignore */\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Reads and removes the stashed target. Always consumed, even when stale. */\\r\\nexport function consumeStashedReturnTo(): string | null {\\r\\n  if (typeof window === \\"undefined\\") return null;\\r\\n  let raw: string | null = null;\\r\\n  let ts = 0;\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    raw = sessionStorage.getItem(STASH_KEY);\\r\\n    ts = Number(sessionStorage.getItem(STASH_TS_KEY) ?? 0);\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    return null;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  clearStashedReturnTo();\\r\\n  if (!raw) return null;\\r\\n  if (!ts || Date.now() - ts > STASH_MAX_AGE_MS) return null;\\r\\n  return parseSafeReturnTo(raw);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The target for this arrival. The URL wins when it carries one (password\\r\\n * sign-in, the expiry bounce); the stash covers the OAuth round trip, which\\r\\n * comes back with no query of its own.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function resolvePostAuthTarget(): string {\\r\\n  const fromUrl = readReturnToFromUrl();\\r\\n  if (fromUrl) {\\r\\n    clearStashedReturnTo();\\r\\n    return fromUrl;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return consumeStashedReturnTo() ?? DEFAULT_POST_AUTH_TARGET;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Build the /auth URL that preserves where the user was trying to go.\\r\\n * Used by ProtectedRoute and by the session-expiry bounce.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function authUrlWithReturnTo(target: string | null | undefined): string {\\r\\n  const safe = parseSafeReturnTo(target);\\r\\n  return safe ? `/auth?returnTo=${encodeURIComponent(safe)}` : \\"/auth\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Navigate a freshly authenticated user to where they belong.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `replace` defaults to **true**: this always runs while sitting on /auth, and\\r\\n * pushing left the sign-in page in history, one Back press behind a signed-in\\r\\n * user — which then re-redirected them forward. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d19b7c","wall_time_seconds":0.2723035,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3153,"output":"import { useId, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst rooms = [\\r\\n  { film: true, workshop: false, label: \'Film showing. No workshop.\' },\\r\\n  { film: false, workshop: false, label: \'No film. No workshop.\' },\\r\\n  { film: true, workshop: true, label: \'Film showing. Workshop underway.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function NegativeCaseLab() {\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState<number[]>([]);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [workshopAnswer, setWorkshopAnswer] = useState<boolean | null>(null);\\r\\n  const id = useId();\\r\\n  const correct = selected.length === 2 && selected.includes(0) && selected.includes(1);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-negative-lab\\">\\r\\n    <p>Let\'s take the same reasoning into a sentence whose required condition contains a negative. Accept this rule for the exercise:</p>\\r\\n    <blockquote>If a room is showing a film, it is not hosting a workshop.</blockquote>\\r\\n    <p>Use <strong>F</strong> for showing a film and <strong>W</strong> for hosting a workshop. Our rule is <strong>F → ~W</strong>. A film requires the absence of a workshop. We haven\'t said a film is the only reason a room might have no workshop.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-given-fact\\"><span className=\\"co-kicker\\">Given fact</span><strong>This room is not hosting a workshop: ~W.</strong></div>\\r\\n    <h3 id={id}>Which possible rooms fit both the rule and that fact? Select every one that fits.</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-room-options\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={id}>{rooms.map((room, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={room.label} aria-pressed={selected.includes(index)} onClick={() => { setSelected(items => items.includes(index) ? items.filter(i => i !== index) : [...items, index]); setChecked(false); }}><span className=\\"co-room-drawing\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><i data-lit={room.film}/><b data-active={room.workshop}>W</b></span><span>{room.label}</span></button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={selected.length === 0} onClick={() => setChecked(true)}>Check these possibilities</button>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className=\\"co-feedback\\"><strong>{correct ? \'Both film possibilities remain open.\' : selected.includes(2) ? \'A workshop conflicts with the given fact.\' : \'There is another case that fits.\'}</strong><p>{selected.includes(2) ? \\"The fact says no workshop. A film and a workshop would also break the original rule. Remove that room, then check the others.\\" : \\"A film with no workshop fits. No film with no workshop fits too. Both keep the rule true and satisfy the given fact, so ~W establishes neither F nor ~F. We know there is no workshop; we don\'t know whether there is a film.\\"}</p></div>}</div>\\r\\n    {checked && correct && <div className=\\"co-case-takeaway\\"><h3>Change the fact: now a workshop is underway.</h3><p>The rule stays F → ~W. Could this room also be showing a film?</p><div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={workshopAnswer === true} onClick={() => setWorkshopAnswer(true)}>Yes, it could.</button><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={workshopAnswer === false} onClick={() => setWorkshopAnswer(false)}>No, it could not.</button></div><div role=\\"status\\">{workshopAnswer !== null && <p>{workshopAnswer ? \\"A film would require no workshop. That conflicts with our new fact that a workshop is underway, so a film is ruled out.\\" : \\"A film would require no workshop, but a workshop is underway. That rules out a film. In symbols, W → ~F. Negating ~W gives W, which is why this is the contrapositive.\\"}</p>}</div></div>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst comparisons = [\\r\\n  { sentence: \'Only stamped envelopes go into the dispatch tray.\', same: true, reason: \'Only makes a stamp necessary for an envelope in the tray. An envelope in the tray must be stamped. A stamped envelope can still be elsewhere.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray if it is stamped.\', same: false, reason: \'If now makes the stamp sufficient for going into the tray. This sends every stamped envelope there. The original rule allowed stamped envelopes elsewhere.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'The only envelopes in the dispatch tray are stamped envelopes.\', same: true, reason: \'The only refers to envelopes in the tray. Being in the tray is enough to establish a stamp, just as in the original rule.\' },\\r\\n  { sentence: \'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray only if it is stamped.\', same: true, reason: \'Only if makes being stamped a requirement for going into the tray. It does not promise a place in the tray to every stamped envelope.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function RuleComparisonLab() {\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Record<number, boolean>>({});\\r\\n  const [explain, setExplain] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const question = useId();\\r\\n  const item = comparisons[index];\\r\\n  const answer = answers[index];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-comparison-lab\\">\\r\\n    <p>You now know both groups. Set the group names aside and read what each sentence promises.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Keep this original rule</p><blockquote>Every envelope in the dispatch tray is stamped.</blockquote>\\r\\n    <p>An envelope in the tray has to be stamped. A stamped envelope could still be elsewhere. Use that possible case to check each new version.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-word-tabs\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Wording variations\\">{comparisons.map((_, i) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={i} aria-pressed={index === i} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setExplain(false); }}>Version {i + 1}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><h3 id={question}>Does this preserve the original rule, or reverse it?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={question}><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={answer === true} onClick={() => setAnswers({ ...answers, [index]: true })}>Same rule</button><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={answer === false} onClick={() => setAnswers({ ...answers, [index]: false })}>Reversed rule</button></div>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{answer !== undefined && <div className=\\"co-feedback\\"><strong>{answer === item.same ? \'The meaning matches your choice.\' : \'Read the starting condition again.\'}</strong><p>{item.reason}</p></div>}</div>\\r\\n    {Object.keys(answers).length === comparisons.length && <div className=\\"co-case-takeaway\\"><h3>One word can change what follows</h3><p>Compare versions 2 and 4. Before opening the explanation, say what adding only did to the role of the stamp.</p><button type=\\"button\\" aria-expanded={explain} onClick={() => setExplain(!explain)}>Compare if with only if</button>{explain && <p>If makes the stamp enough for going into the tray. Only if makes it required for going into the tray. The stamp moved from the sufficient role to the necessary role. That\'s why reading the complete expression matters.</p>}</div>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// Original application of the Demon clips\' concrete-to-abstract and case-testing approach.\\r\\nexport function CounterexampleLab() {\\r\\n  const [drawer, setDrawer] = useState<boolean | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [border, setBorder] = useState<boolean | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [found, setFound] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const id = useId();\\r\\n  const fits = !drawer || border;\\r\\n  const refutes = drawer === false && border === true;\\r\\n  function change(which: \'drawer\' | \'border\', value: boolean) { (which === \'drawer\' ? setDrawer : setBorder)(value); setChecked(false); }\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-case-maker\\">\\r\\n    <p>So far, we\'ve explained why an arrow cannot simply be reversed. Now make that limit visible.</p>\\r\\n    <blockquote>Every ticket in Drawer R has a blue border.</blockquote>\\r\\n    <p>Accept that rule for this exercise. We don\'t need to know why the tickets were arranged that way. A ticket in Drawer R must have a blue border.</p>\\r\\n    <h3 id={id}>Someone claims: every blue-bordered ticket is in Drawer R. Can you build one ticket that fits the rule but disproves that claim?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-case-workbench\\">\\r\\n      <div className=\\"co-ticket\\" data-blue={border === true} aria-label={`Your ticket: ${drawer === null ? \'location undecided\' : drawer ? \'in Drawer R\' : \'outside Drawer R\'}; ${border === null ? \'border undecided\' : border ? \'blue border\' : \'no blue border\'}`}><span>ADMIT ONE</span><strong>R / 017</strong><span>{drawer === null ? \'Choose a location\' : drawer ? \'In Drawer R\' : \'Outside Drawer R\'}</span><small>{border === null ? \'Choose a border\' : border ? \'Blue border\' : \'No blue border\'}</small></div>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"co-case-decisions\\" aria-describedby={id}>\\r\\n        <fieldset><legend>Where is the ticket?</legend><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={drawer === true} onClick={() => change(\'drawer\', true)}>In Drawer R</button><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={drawer === false} onClick={() => change(\'drawer\', false)}>Outside Drawer R</button></fieldset>\\r\\n        <fieldset><legend>What border does it have?</legend><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={border === true} onClick={() => change(\'border\', true)}>Blue border</button><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={border === false} onClick={() => change(\'border\', false)}>No blue border</button></fieldset>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={drawer === null || border === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); if (refutes) setFound(true); }}>Test this ticket</button>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className=\\"co-feedback\\"><strong>{!fits ? \'This ticket breaks the original rule.\' : refutes ? \'This fits the rule and disproves the reverse.\' : \'This fits the rule. Does it disprove the claim?\'}</strong><p>{!fits ? \\"A ticket in Drawer R has to have a blue border. We need a case the rule allows, so this cannot serve as our example.\\" : refutes ? \\"The ticket has a blue border but stays outside Drawer R. Nothing in the original rule forbids that. The reversed claim forbids it, so the two statements cannot mean the same thing.\\" : drawer ? \\"A blue-bordered ticket inside Drawer R fits both statements. To show that the reverse adds a claim, try keeping the blue border and changing the location.\\" : \\"A ticket without a blue border outside Drawer R fits both statements. The reverse makes a claim about blue-bordered tickets. Build one of those and see whether it has to be in Drawer R.\\"}</p></div>}</div>\\r\\n    {found && <div className=\\"co-case-takeaway\\"><h3>Now explain the difference</h3><p>The rule limits tickets <strong>inside the drawer</strong>. It doesn\'t put every ticket with a blue border there. A possible case is enough to show that the reverse doesn\'t follow; we\'re not claiming that this ticket actually exists.</p><p>Without looking back, say what changed when you reversed the rule.</p></div>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { DIAGRAM_ITEMS, type DiagramItem } from \'@/features/conditional-refresher/content\';\\r\\n\\r\\n// Reviewed individually for the opening after both indicator families. Keep\\r\\n// source IDs and sentences stable; never select by group alone, since groups\\r\\n// also contain compound conditions that this opening has not taught.\\r\\nexport const openingActivityIds = [\'d1\', \'d2\', \'d3\', \'d5\', \'d6\', \'d7\', \'d8\'] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const openingActivityItems: DiagramItem[] = openingActivityIds.map(id => {\\r\\n  const item = DIAGRAM_ITEMS.find(candidate => candidate.id === id);\\r\\n  if (!item) throw new Error(`Missing reviewed opening Activity item: ${id}`);\\r\\n  return item;\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const openingActivityExplanations: Record<(typeof openingActivityIds)[number], string> = {\\r\\n  d1: \\"If Mia trains, she qualifies. Training is enough under this rule. Qualifying alone doesn\'t tell us whether she trains.\\",\\r\\n  d2: \\"If someone is a dancer, that person stretches. The rule covers every dancer. It doesn\'t say that everyone who stretches is a dancer.\\",\\r\\n  d3: \\"If a student is admitted, that student is a senior. An admitted student who isn\'t a senior would break the rule. Being a senior alone doesn\'t establish admission.\\",\\r\\n  d5: \\"If someone votes, that person is a member. Membership is required for voting. The sentence doesn\'t say that every member votes.\\",\\r\\n  d6: \\"If the bridge opens, it has been inspected. Inspection is required for opening. An inspected bridge could still stay closed.\\",\\r\\n  d7: \\"If there is funding, there is approval. The sentence makes approval a requirement for funding. Approval alone doesn\'t establish funding.\\",\\r\\n  d8: \\"If someone is a hiker, that person brings a map. Here, \'always\' states what hikers do. It doesn\'t merely tell them what they ought to do, and bringing a map alone doesn\'t establish that someone is a hiker.\\",\\r\\n};\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/mixedDrillItems.ts')\np.write_text('''/** Reviewed single-rule practice after Groups 1 and 2, before contraposition. */\nexport type MixedDrillError = 'direction' | 'qualification' | 'requirement' | 'equivalence';\n\nexport type MixedDrillChoiceItem = {\n  id: string;\n  stage: 'complete-condition' | 'equivalence' | 'transfer' | 'confirmation';\n  skill: MixedDrillError;\n  sentence: string;\n  prompt: string;\n  options: string[];\n  correct: number;\n  feedback: string[];\n};\n\nexport const completeConditionItems: MixedDrillChoiceItem[] = [\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-condition-1', stage: 'complete-condition', skill: 'qualification',\n    sentence: 'Every swimmer training before dawn receives a wristband.',\n    prompt: 'What complete condition does every refer to?',\n    options: ['Is a swimmer', 'Is a swimmer training before dawn', 'Receives a wristband'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'Swimmer alone includes people training later in the day. The rule covers swimmers training before dawn, so keep the time restriction.',\n      'Every covers each swimmer training before dawn. That complete condition is enough to establish a wristband. We have not been told what later swimmers receive.',\n      'The wristband is what the rule establishes. Every refers to the swimmers training before dawn, not to everyone who receives a wristband.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-condition-2', stage: 'complete-condition', skill: 'qualification',\n    sentence: 'Only locker keys with yellow tags open the cabinet.',\n    prompt: 'What complete condition does only identify as required?',\n    options: ['Is a locker key', 'Opens the cabinet', 'Is a locker key with a yellow tag'], correct: 2,\n    feedback: [\n      'A locker key without a yellow tag does not meet this requirement. Keep with a yellow tag attached to locker key.',\n      'Opening the cabinet is what needs the required kind of key. Only identifies a locker key with a yellow tag as the requirement.',\n      'A key that opens the cabinet has to be a locker key with a yellow tag. That whole description is required. We have not been told that every yellow-tagged locker key opens it.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-condition-3', stage: 'complete-condition', skill: 'qualification',\n    sentence: 'A current safety certificate is required to lead the climbing group.',\n    prompt: 'Which complete requirement must your label preserve?',\n    options: ['Has a current safety certificate', 'Has a safety certificate', 'Leads the climbing group'], correct: 0,\n    feedback: [\n      'Current belongs in the requirement. Leading the climbing group requires a current safety certificate; an expired certificate would not meet the stated requirement. Having a current one does not establish that someone leads the group.',\n      'Safety certificate alone allows an expired certificate. The sentence requires a current one, so that qualification must stay in the label.',\n      'Leading the group is what needs the certificate. Required refers back to the complete requirement before it: a current safety certificate.',\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nconst envelopeRule = 'Every envelope in the dispatch tray is stamped.';\n\n/** The four reviewed comparisons retain the existing envelope example. */\nexport const equivalenceItems: MixedDrillChoiceItem[] = [\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-equivalence-1', stage: 'equivalence', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'Only stamped envelopes go into the dispatch tray.',\n    prompt: `Original rule: ${envelopeRule} Does the new sentence preserve it?`,\n    options: ['Same rule', 'Reversed rule'], correct: 0,\n    feedback: [\n      'Only makes a stamp necessary for an envelope in the tray. An envelope in the tray must be stamped, just as in the original. A stamped envelope can still be elsewhere.',\n      'This has not made a stamp enough for going into the tray. Only limits tray envelopes to stamped ones, so the starting condition remains being in the tray.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-equivalence-2', stage: 'equivalence', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray if it is stamped.',\n    prompt: `Original rule: ${envelopeRule} Does the new sentence preserve it?`,\n    options: ['Same rule', 'Reversed rule'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'If now makes a stamp sufficient for going into the tray. The original allowed a stamped envelope elsewhere. This sentence sends every stamped envelope into the tray, so it changes the rule.',\n      'The new sentence starts from the stamp and establishes the tray. The original starts from the tray and establishes the stamp. A stamped envelope elsewhere fits the original but conflicts with this reverse.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-equivalence-3', stage: 'equivalence', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'The only envelopes in the dispatch tray are stamped envelopes.',\n    prompt: `Original rule: ${envelopeRule} Does the new sentence preserve it?`,\n    options: ['Reversed rule', 'Same rule'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'The only refers to envelopes in the dispatch tray. Pick an envelope in that group and it must be stamped. Starting from a stamp instead would add a claim about where stamped envelopes go.',\n      'The only identifies the tray envelopes as the complete starting group. Each has a stamp. This keeps the original relationship and leaves stamped envelopes elsewhere possible.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-equivalence-4', stage: 'equivalence', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray only if it is stamped.',\n    prompt: `Original rule: ${envelopeRule} Does the new sentence preserve it?`,\n    options: ['Same rule', 'Reversed rule'], correct: 0,\n    feedback: [\n      'Only if makes being stamped a requirement for going into the tray. Every envelope in the tray must meet that requirement. It does not promise a place there to every stamped envelope.',\n      'Read only if as the whole expression. The stamp is required for the tray, rather than enough for it. That preserves the original rule.',\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nexport const transferItems: MixedDrillChoiceItem[] = [\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-transfer-1', stage: 'transfer', skill: 'direction',\n    sentence: 'The heater runs if the timer reaches six.',\n    prompt: 'Which diagram preserves what the sentence says?',\n    options: ['Heater runs → Timer reaches six', 'Timer reaches six → Heater runs', 'Timer has not reached six → Heater does not run'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'The sentence has not said the timer is the only way to start the heater. A running heater before six could fit the rule. Start from the complete if-clause: the timer reaches six.',\n      'The if-clause is enough even though it comes last. If the timer reaches six, the heater runs. A running heater alone leaves the timer reading open.',\n      'You made the absence of six enough to establish that the heater is off. It could run at another time without breaking the rule. The sentence only establishes running when the timer reaches six.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-transfer-2', stage: 'transfer', skill: 'qualification',\n    sentence: 'Any trainee certified for night work receives a shift badge.',\n    prompt: 'Which diagram keeps the complete conditions?',\n    options: ['Trainee → Receives shift badge', 'Receives shift badge → Trainee certified for night work', 'Trainee certified for night work → Receives shift badge'], correct: 2,\n    feedback: [\n      'Trainee alone extends the rule to trainees without night-work certification. Keep certified for night work in the starting condition.',\n      'Receiving the badge is the result, not proof of this certification. A badge recipient without that certification could fit the rule. Start from the complete group the sentence covers.',\n      'The rule covers every trainee certified for night work. That full condition establishes a shift badge. It leaves the badge status of other trainees open.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-transfer-3', stage: 'transfer', skill: 'requirement',\n    sentence: 'Only electric vehicles are permitted inside the courtyard.',\n    prompt: 'A vehicle is electric. What does this rule establish about its permission to enter?',\n    options: ['It is permitted inside.', 'It is not permitted inside.', 'Its permission remains open.'], correct: 2,\n    feedback: [\n      'Electric is required for permission, but the sentence does not make it enough. An electric vehicle denied permission still fits the rule. Actual entry is a separate question.',\n      'The rule does not deny permission to electric vehicles. Some could be permitted and others denied. It only restricts permitted vehicles to electric ones.',\n      'Any permitted vehicle has to be electric. Knowing a vehicle is electric does not settle whether it has permission. An electric vehicle denied permission fits the sentence, as does an electric vehicle with permission.',\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    id: 'mixed-transfer-4', stage: 'transfer', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'Finalists must attend the safety interview.',\n    prompt: 'Someone rewrites this as: Every finalist attended the safety interview. Does the rewrite keep the original meaning?',\n    options: ['Yes. A requirement proves that the action happened.', 'No. The rewrite reports attendance instead of an obligation.'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'Must gives finalists an obligation to attend. It does not supply a fact showing they complied. Keep required to attend in the result rather than reporting completed attendance.',\n      'The original establishes an obligation for each finalist. A finalist who has not attended could still have that obligation. The rewrite reports completed attendance, so it adds something the original did not establish.',\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\n/** New cases confirm a repaired skill without repeating the question just solved. */\nexport const confirmationItemsByError: Record<MixedDrillError, MixedDrillChoiceItem[]> = {\n  direction: [{\n    id: 'mixed-confirm-direction', stage: 'confirmation', skill: 'direction',\n    sentence: 'An invitation is printed when the registration is accepted.',\n    prompt: 'Which condition is enough to establish the other under this rule?',\n    options: ['An invitation is printed', 'The registration is accepted'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'Printing is what acceptance establishes. The rule has not said every printed invitation proves acceptance. Start from the complete when-clause.',\n      'When identifies acceptance as enough for printing. Acceptance establishes the invitation. A printed invitation alone leaves acceptance open.',\n    ],\n  }],\n  qualification: [{\n    id: 'mixed-confirm-qualification', stage: 'confirmation', skill: 'qualification',\n    sentence: 'Every courier delivering refrigerated medicine carries an insulated pack.',\n    prompt: 'Which starting label keeps the full group covered by every?',\n    options: ['Courier delivering refrigerated medicine', 'Courier', 'Courier delivering medicine'], correct: 0,\n    feedback: [\n      'Keep delivering refrigerated medicine attached to courier. The rule establishes an insulated pack for each courier in that complete group. It leaves other couriers outside this promise.',\n      'Courier alone includes couriers carrying other deliveries. The rule covers couriers delivering refrigerated medicine, so retain both delivery and refrigeration.',\n      'Medicine alone includes medicine that is not refrigerated. That would broaden the group beyond the sentence. Keep refrigerated in the complete condition.',\n    ],\n  }],\n  requirement: [{\n    id: 'mixed-confirm-requirement', stage: 'confirmation', skill: 'requirement',\n    sentence: 'A reader books a study room only if the reader has a verified account.',\n    prompt: 'A reader has a verified account. Does the rule establish a booking?',\n    options: ['No. A booking remains open.', 'Yes. Verification is enough for a booking.'], correct: 0,\n    feedback: [\n      'Only if makes verification required for booking. A reader with a verified account could make a booking or never make one. Verification alone leaves booking open.',\n      'You made the requirement enough. A verified reader who does not book fits the sentence. The rule tells us what a booking needs, not that verification establishes booking.',\n    ],\n  }],\n  equivalence: [{\n    id: 'mixed-confirm-equivalence', stage: 'confirmation', skill: 'equivalence',\n    sentence: 'Only registered boats use the slipway.',\n    prompt: 'Does this say the same thing as: Every boat using the slipway is registered?',\n    options: ['No. The second sentence reverses the rule.', 'Yes. Both require registration for using the slipway.'], correct: 1,\n    feedback: [\n      'Only restricts slipway users to registered boats. Every boat using the slipway must therefore be registered. Both start from use, rather than from registration.',\n      'Both require registration for a boat using the slipway. A registered boat that never uses it fits both sentences. Neither makes registration enough for use.',\n    ],\n  }],\n};\n''',encoding='utf-8')\n'@ | python -","workdir":"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp","max_output_tokens":1000}));

Finding that idea matters more than taking the next few words in the sentence.</p>\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:823:        <p>A useful way to read the whole rule is: <strong>the left condition requires the right condition</strong>. Then place it on its side of the arrow and read the relationship in ordinary English.</p>\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:856:        <p>The only what? If someone is admitted, the sentence tells us they\'re a member.</p>\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:870:      <p>If you know the meaning but hesitate over the arrow, imagine the left condition is true. title.split(\':\')[0] : ({\\r\\n    \'Find the target, then place it\': \'Find the target\',\\r\\n    \'Find the requirement, then place it\': \'Find the requirement\',\\r\\n    \'Try the sentence from the inside\': \'Personalized If/Then\',\\r\\n    \'A guarantee is stronger than a tendency\': \'Guarantees and wording\',\\r\\n    \'Having a requirement does not guarantee the result\': \'Requirement or guarantee?\',\\r\\n    \'Try the same reasoning with a new rule\': \'Try a new rule\',\\r\\n    \'Say the rule without the clues\': \'Recall the rule\',\\r\\n  }[title] || title);\\r\\n  return <section id={id || `co-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \'-\')}`} className={`co-section ${dark ? \'Hide the reasoning\' : \'Show the reasoning\'}</button><div id={answerId} aria-live=\\"polite\\">{revealed && <div className=\\"co-pause-answer\\">{children}</div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Check({ prompt, options, correct, feedback }: { prompt: string; options: string[]; correct: number; feedback: string[] }) {\\r\\n  const [choice, setChoice] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  if (!Array.isArray(feedback) || feedback.length !== options.length || feedback.some(message => typeof message !== \'string\' || !message.trim())) {\\r\\n    throw new Error(`Every answer choice needs an explanation: ${prompt}`);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-check\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Try it</p><h3>{prompt}</h3><div className=\\"co-choices\\">{options.map((option, i) => <button key={option} type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={choice === i} onClick={() => setChoice(i)}>{option}</button>)}</div><div role=\\"status\\">{choice !== null && <div className={`co-feedback ${choice === correct ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`} ><span className=\\"co-answer-state\\">{choice === correct ? \'Correct.\' : \'Not quite.\'}</span><strong>Your choice: {options[choice]}</strong><p>{feedback[choice]}</p></div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction GateExplorer() {\\r\\n  const [fact, setFact] = useState<\'win\' | \'bicycle\'>(\'win\');\\r\\n"}'}]
