text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('wiki/hot.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\nnewblock='''## Most recent (2026-10-07): Conditional opening recovered and refined locally\n\n- Recovered the missing conditional checkout at `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp`. All 47 recovered final source files matched their preservation hashes before edits. Main checkout remains separate. [Prior audit and integration map](../brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/2026-10-03-astra-learning-review.md).\n- Implemented the six audited corrections locally: permission rather than non-entry counterexample; three fresh negative originals after the six preserved final items; independent written translation before supplied labels; explicit mixed practice progression; three-compatible/one-violating case recap; accurate hidden-family wording. Nine notes, four labs and 25 cards retained.\n- Joshua then replaced the three-section mixed lab with one question at a time and a progress bar. Current local mixed drill has 24 questions in five stages: complete conditions, seven reviewed diagrams, six independent translations, four meaning comparisons and four transfer cases. Wrong answers lead to specific feedback, revision and a fresh confirmation for the distinction missed.\n- Existing Activity reuse is limited to reviewed single-rule diagram IDs d1/d2/d3/d5/d6/d7/d8. Original recorded practice, assignment and history contracts remain separate; the opening's formative runner is session-local and does not claim saved student results.\n- Joshua rejected generic headings and instructions that name an operation without showing why it matters. Copy now asks concrete questions about reports arriving before noon, current passes, wristbands and yellow-tagged keys. [Copy corrections](COPY-CORRECTIONS.md) records the demonstrated failures; no claim of an objectively identical voice.\n- The local load failure was a missing preview import, not a reason to bypass access. Preview preflight and safe lazy loading repaired that path. The signed-out BootcampGate now follows the normal auth return flow, retaining the full route/query/hash; signed-in assignment enforcement is preserved. Parent browser verified the normal lesson route reaches auth.\n- Preview remains on port 8097 in the recovered checkout. [Local opening](http://127.0.0.1:8097/dev/conditional-opening-preview). No commit, push, production merge, publication, backend grant or Lovable credit spend in this work.\n- Final combined tests, typecheck/build and latest responsive interaction verification are pending the parent result at this handoff write. Earlier scoped passes do not certify the final edited tree. Next: record the final verification, review the local result, then assess a production merge separately. Task statuses unchanged.\n\n'''\noldheading='## Most recent (2026-10-07): Open-app recovery and account setup'\nassert oldheading in original\nreplacement=original.replace(oldheading, newblock+'## Previous (2026-10-07): Open-app recovery and account setup',1)\nreplacement=replacement.replace('- **Conditional opening:** local Astra page/card implementation and host/browser checks are complete within the bounds above. Obtain fresh Claude inspection when the native service permits it. Broader curriculum, media and production acceptance remain open; [[tasks]] is unchanged.', '- **Conditional opening:** recovered local checkout now includes the audit fixes and Joshua\\'s five-stage mixed drill. Final checks are pending the current parent result; production merge and publication have not started. Broader curriculum, media and signed-in production acceptance remain open; [[tasks]] is unchanged.',1)\nreplacement=replacement.replace('- Conditional opening: fresh independent Claude inspection remains blocked by the session limit reported above. Preserve local work and the failed result records; do not treat older approval as current. Final local viewport proof is complete within the stated bounds. No publication or live student-persistence claim.', '- Conditional opening: preserve the recovered checkout and current preview on 8097. Record the final current-tree tests and browser evidence before a production merge. Older Claude approval and viewport reports remain historical; no current independent Claude certification, publication or live student-persistence claim.',1)\nassert p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')==original\np.write_text(replacement,encoding='utf-8')\np=Path('wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\nentry='''\n\n### Conditional practice: explain the decision instead of naming the operation (2026-10-07)\n\nJoshua rejected “same rule, different words,” “equivalent-rule comparison,” “find the requirement, then place it,” “work through the expressions below,” “first choose the complete target,” “read the whole idea,” and “sentence X of Y” when these stood in for a useful explanation or made the drill feel like a collection of labeled sections. “Complete target” and similar terminology should not carry a beginner's instructions without a concrete meaning attached.\n\nThe revised local copy asks what the learner needs to decide: “Who does the rule say will receive a wristband?”, “What kind of key is needed to open the cabinet?”, and “What kind of pass does entry require?” The lesson demonstrates why before noon, current, registered and with yellow tags matter. Dropping one of those words changes which cases the rule covers. An arrow is checked by imagining its left condition and asking what the sentence then establishes, rather than treating the first English clause as the left side.\n\nJoshua also asked for the mixed drill to train a skill and build a habit, with one question visible and a progress bar. The current local response uses five stages with a concrete purpose for each: retain the full conditions, build a diagram, attempt a translation before supplied labels, compare meanings, then transfer to unfamiliar cases. Error feedback distinguishes dropped qualifications, reversed roles, required versus enough and changed meaning. A fresh question checks the repaired distinction; correcting the exposed answer alone does not demonstrate transfer. Written translations are compared by the learner and are not represented as automatically graded.\n\nKeep modality in the examples. An electric vehicle that is denied permission disproves the claim that being electric guarantees permission; a vehicle merely staying outside does not. A required action is an obligation, not a fact proving compliance. Confirmation practice for that mistake must test another obligation rather than an unrelated wording comparison.\n\nThese are demonstrated corrections and the current implementation response, not approval of every exact replacement sentence or proof that the voice matches Joshua identically. The [selected voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still governs wording, cadence, concrete reasoning and scope. [Current session record](log.md) distinguishes local work from publication.\n'''\nassert '### Conditional practice: explain the decision instead of naming the operation (2026-10-07)' not in original\nassert p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')==original\np.write_text(original.rstrip()+entry+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\np=Path('wiki/log.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\nentry='''\n\n## [2026-10-07] system | Session wrapup: conditional recovery and five-stage practice\n\n- Source: [prior conditional opening audit and Activity integration map](../brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/2026-10-03-astra-learning-review.md); Joshua's current local-preview corrections. Recovered checkout: `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp`.\n- Recovery: all 47 final recovered source files hash-verified before this session's edits. Main app checkout remained separate. Implemented six audited corrections while retaining nine notes/four labs, 25 cards, six original final items and assignment/history contracts. Three fresh negative originals were appended to the final checkpoint.\n- Practice: Joshua replaced the interim three-round mixed composition with one visible question and a progress bar. Current local runner has 24 questions in five stages, including seven reviewed existing single-rule Activity diagrams (d1/d2/d3/d5/d6/d7/d8), six independent written translations, four envelope comparisons and fresh transfer. Wrong-choice-specific feedback leads to revision and fresh confirmation. Obligation/compliance errors receive a distinct obligation confirmation. This is local formative practice, not a saved or graded production record.\n- Load repair: missing preview import fixed, preflight checks and safe lazy route loading added. Signed-out BootcampGate now redirects through the ordinary auth return URL with full route/query/hash retained. Parent browser verified that normal lesson URL reaches auth. Signed-in assignment checks stay enforced; no permission grant or auth bypass.\n- Changed pages: [hot cache](hot.md) and [copy corrections](COPY-CORRECTIONS.md). Replaced vague operation labels with concrete question wording and developed the reasoning behind full modifiers, enough versus required, permission and obligations. The catalog already contains these pages; their purpose and locations did not change, so no catalog regeneration was needed. Task ledger left unchanged.\n- Verification: final combined current-tree tests, typecheck/build and latest browser matrix are pending the parent result at this record write. Earlier scoped passes remain bounded to their tested snapshots. Preview remains on port 8097: [local opening](http://127.0.0.1:8097/dev/conditional-opening-preview).\n- Open/skipped: production merge, commit/push/publication and signed-in production persistence verification not started. No backend write, real assignment, notification or Lovable credit spend. No claim of identical Joshua voice or whole-course completion. Preserve unrelated files and older recovery/audit evidence.\n'''\nassert '## [2026-10-07] system | Session wrapup: conditional recovery and five-stage practice' not in original\nassert p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')==original\np.write_text(original.rstrip()+entry+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\n'@ | python -","workdir":"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os","max_output_tokens":1000}));

It deliberately omits assignment and account persistence. The proper local LSAT U course is http://127.0.0.1:8097/bootcamp/conditionals/lessons and uses the normal sign-in, teacher assignment gates and saved progress. Local source changes are not published to lsatprep.study by starting either route.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe existing BootcampGate can show “Not assigned yet” when signed out as well as when a signed-in student lacks access. That screen alone does not verify authentication or assignment state. No access grants or student assignments were changed to make the preview work.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerification on 2026-10-07: Node syntax check and scoped diff whitespace check passed. An unknown launch argument failed immediately. The real preflight caught seven TypeScript errors in the in-progress Activity test file and exited 1 without starting Vite. A successful launch still requires those errors to be corrected and preflight to pass; the failed preflight is evidence that incomplete changes do not start this launcher.\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\naddition='''\n\nFurther steering in the same session: “Keep the complete condition” was itself awkward as a visible heading. Ask who or what the particular rule covers instead. Joshua also rejected the landing page's total question/stage counts, per-stage counts, long stage list and padded explanation of repairs. A shorter launch should explain the immediate task; each stage should introduce its purpose and instructions when the learner reaches it. That latest flow revision was requested while final verification was still pending, so this record does not certify its implementation.\n'''\nassert p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')==original\np.write_text(original.rstrip()+addition+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\np=Path('wiki/hot.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\nold='- Final combined tests, typecheck/build and latest responsive interaction verification are pending the parent result at this handoff write. Earlier scoped passes do not certify the final edited tree. Next: record the final verification, review the local result, then assess a production merge separately. Task statuses unchanged.'\nnew='- Latest steering asks for a shorter drill landing without question/stage totals or a long list, with a purpose-and-instructions pause at each stage. That revision and final combined checks are pending the parent result at this handoff write; earlier scoped passes do not certify the final tree. Browser work is restricted to the Codex in-app browser. Next: record final verification and review the local result before assessing a production merge. Task statuses unchanged.'\nassert old in original\nreplacement=original.replace(old,new,1)\nassert p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')==original\np.write_text(replacement,encoding='utf-8')\np=Path('wiki/log.md')\noriginal=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\naddition='''\n\n### Conditional refinement steering received before final checks\n\nJoshua requested removal of the landing's question/stage counts and long stage list, a purpose-and-instructions pause at each stage, and concrete wording about who or what a rule covers in place of “Keep the complete condition.” Current revisions and final verification remain pending. Browser work is limited to the Codex in-app browser. Fresh confirmation cases are distinct from the exposed answer. An obligation/compliance error receives another obligation case, instead of an unrelated general equivalence question. Startup preflight and safe lazy import recovery address that local load path. BootcampGate now redirects a signed-out visitor through the normal auth return URL, retaining route, query and hash; it still enforces assignments for signed-in students. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b7c77e","wall_time_seconds":0.8970142,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3975,"output":"---\\r\\nname: audit\\r\\ndescription: Use when someone asks to audit their AIOS, score the Four Cs, find stale paths or unlinked projects, compare AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md, check Claude/Codex skill compatibility, or assess migration readiness. Automatically saves dated reports and tracks evidence-backed improvements across runs.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# AIOS Audit\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck whether this AIOS can find the right information and do useful work reliably, and identify changes that would make the user\'s work easier. Score **Context, Connections, Capabilities, and Cadence out of 25 each**, using [rubric.md](rubric.md). This is rubric **v2**. Label the number **verified operational reliability**, not overall usefulness or percentage of work the AIOS can do. A folder, installed skill, API key, or confident claim is not proof of a working system.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun locally in the current project. Do not launch subagents unless the user requests them. Audit first; `/link` repairs a route, and `/level-up` helps close one meaningful gap. Never inflate or deliberately depress a score to encourage another run. Improvement comes from better evidence and behavior.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Every audit automatically saves its report in the audited project\'s `audits/` folder.** Read [history.md](history.md) for prior-report selection, finding transitions, comparison rules, and safe persistence. Saving the local audit record is part of this skill, not a separate approval step. An explicit user request not to save overrides this default. The inspected system remains unchanged.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Establish scope and evidence\\r\\n\\r\\n- Get the current date and project root. Read applicable `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and local overrides; follow the runtime\'s instruction hierarchy. Read both manuals when both exist. Check for conflicting routing and promised synchronization, without assuming distinct runtime instructions must be identical.\\r\\n- Load the relevant prior AIOS report and carried finding ledger using [history.md](history.md), before selecting probes. Recheck important prior findings alongside current priorities; do not assume earlier results remain true. Identify the comparison baseline and scope changes before scoring.\\r\\n- Use the manual\'s routing rules first. Read its project and knowledge indexes before listing their immediate child folders. Inspect only relevant scoped manuals. Skip dependencies, caches, generated output, and archives unless a route points there or an archived/current ambiguity needs checking.\\r\\n- Identify the user\'s role, main objective, current priorities, and up to three important workflows from actual context. Unfilled template placeholders do not count. Do not require business revenue data from someone whose work has no revenue function.\\r\\n- Inspect the supported skill/agent registries, connection references, and actual scheduler configuration/run evidence. Accept scripts, MCPs, exports, local data, and equivalent mechanisms equally. Count inventories for orientation only.\\r\\n- Record each finding as **verified**, **documented but unverified**, **missing**, **stale**, or **conflicting**, with a file/line, source URL, or dated run reference. File modification times and last-checked labels alone do not prove a successful refresh or execution.\\r\\n- Start with targeted reads. If a time budget or local page limit prevents verification, mark it unverified and state coverage. Never award full points because there was no time to check. Do not claim an exhaustive audit from a sample.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Test routing and context retrieval\\r\\n\\r\\nMake a compact map: **user need → manual/index → specific source → freshness rule**. Include applicable business context, people, priorities/commitments, original records, project deliverables, specialist knowledge bases, media/assets, and external systems. A generic folder or provider name is insufficient when it leaves the relevant source unclear.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompare the maps with immediate folders to find important unindexed projects, outdated counts, broken paths, ambiguous aliases, duplicate sources of truth, and retired material presented as active. Distinguish confirmed gaps from folders whose relevance is unknown. Check whether supplied routes work from the current checkout, including references inside skills and generated mirrors where that runtime uses them.\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow the declared routes for these five questions, adapted to the user\'s real work:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. What does this person/business do, for whom, and what matters now?\\r\\n2. Where is the authoritative current priority, commitment, or status, and how would I verify it?\\r\\n3. Where is the latest deliverable and next step for an important active project?\\r\\n4. Where is a previous decision, lesson, or subject-specific knowledge item and its supporting source?\\r\\n5. Where is one commonly needed external record, asset, or original document, and how is it accessed?\\r\\n\\r\\nSelect relevant examples before searching for answers. If a category does not apply, record why and substitute a different real retrieval need, keeping five probes. For each, show the question, route attempted, source, result, and whether it was found directly, only by fallback search, or not found. A broad search can recover an answer, but it does not prove the declared route works. Mark external access unverified if not checked; a documented access path earns only documentation credit. Score only the evidence actually observed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check freshness and authority explicitly:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- An optional `_hot.md`, briefing, or cache earns no points just for existing. Do not recommend creating one by default. Check whether it duplicates canonical pages, has a real refresh mechanism, and presents stale numbers or past deadlines as current. A recently edited header does not refresh every fact inside it.\\r\\n- If a cache adds duplication without reliable upkeep, recommend bypassing it in retrieval and using the relevant index/source directly. Never automatically remove it. No cache is a valid architecture.\\r\\n- Stable context can live in a wiki; current metrics and task status should resolve to the appropriate live system or dated export. Exact terms should resolve to original records. Check source dates and flag conflicts rather than treating the wiki as automatically authoritative for every type of fact.\\r\\n- Check whether a fresh session could understand the user, resume the selected project, and find evidence without depending on this conversation. Praise compact, accurate routing; do not reward manual length, exhaustive root catalogs, or reading every wiki page.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Verify the other three Cs\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst run the required [routing and cross-runtime compatibility checks](compatibility.md). This covers stale/broken paths, important unlinked work, differences between operating manuals, and skill availability across the runtimes in use or targeted for migration. Compare shared behavior and required resources, not raw file equality alone. Record expected runtime differences separately from defects. The protocol also defines the mandatory compatibility table, evidence labels, and calibration cases.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Connections:** Identify applicable domains: finances, customer interactions, calendar, communication, tasks, meetings, and knowledge/files; add content or specialist domains when material. Record each domain\'s relevance, mechanism, specific access route, successful-read evidence, date, and limitations. One tool may cover multiple domains only when each has evidence. A configured MCP or `.env` key does not establish authentication. Use narrow, safe read-only checks when available, or dated successful-run evidence within the source\'s expected refresh interval. With no documented interval, use 30 days and explain why highly volatile data may require a fresh read. Do not print secret values or bulk private records. Do not run unknown scripts before checking their side effects.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Capabilities:** Select up to three workflows tied to the identified priorities, not merely the most polished demos. Examine triggers, inputs, output destination, examples of actual usable outputs, verification, failure handling, and repeated use. Missing workflows remain gaps. Check discoverability, needed supporting files, and canonical/mirror consistency after documented transforms. Agents are optional; quantity, complexity, and custom naming earn no bonus. Use existing outputs or safe bounded checks; do not start paid generation or substantive work just to audit it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cadence:** Inspect actual enabled schedules, event triggers, hooks, or explicitly defined human-run rituals and their execution records. Identify the host/runtime, trigger, expected output, last due execution, success/failure evidence, and stop/recovery controls. A skill named `daily-*`, a template, or recently edited files does not establish cadence. A useful manual ritual gets limited credit; label it manual. Do not call a local job unattended or laptop-independent without evidence for that environment. Judge monthly or quarterly routines against their real due dates rather than a fixed weekly window.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Score and prioritize\\r\\n\\r\\nRead the complete [rubric.md](rubric.md). Assign all 20 criterion scores, then apply its caps. Show the four subtotals, raw sum, any cap and reason, final score, and stage. Missing evidence earns no verified credit, but distinguish **unverified** from **known broken**. Record excluded domains with reasons; do not exclude a domain merely because it is disconnected.\\r\\n\\r\\nRank gaps by likely effect on the user\'s work, with wrong/stale answers and missing source access ahead of cosmetic tidiness. Give up to three concrete fixes with an exact affected route/workflow, supporting evidence, and a clear completion check. Do not invent three faults in a healthy system. Distinguish a repair from a check needed to resolve uncertainty.\\r\\n\\r\\nGroup findings into **confirmed defects**, **verification gaps**, and **improvement opportunities**; list intentional differences separately. Keep severity distinct from confidence. Assign each underlying issue a stable ID and reuse it across sections. Do not count one missing mirror or bad route as multiple independent defects. State which existing rubric criteria it affects; do not introduce a migration score or change the v2 anchors. Explain score limitations, including manual-cadence limits when relevant. Unchecked evidence is not proof of failure, and optional enhancements are not defects merely because they are absent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Return a concise, reviewable report\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this structure, keeping the evidence ledger compact:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **AIOS Audit: date, project, rubric v2.** Scope, runtimes, verification limits, and a plain-language conclusion. Lead with what works and the most consequential mismatch. Report unique finding counts by class, not as an exhaustive total outside the inspected scope.\\r\\n2. **AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md findings:** always include the dedicated section specified below, even when no issues are found. Do not bury operating-manual findings in the general fixes or routing table.\\r\\n3. **Routing and migration compatibility:** the coverage/matrix and actionable findings from [compatibility.md](compatibility.md), including confirmed defects, verification gaps, intentional differences, and improvement opportunities. Reference existing finding IDs instead of repeating full manual findings. State whether sampled migration is blocked, needs verification, or passed the inspected checks; do not imply the whole system is portable from a sample.\\r\\n4. **Routing check:** five probe results, including source references and direct/fallback/unresolved status.\\r\\n5. **What works:** up to three evidence-backed strengths.\\r\\n6. **Verified operational reliability:** four rows, each `/25`; raw total, caps, final `/100`, and stage. Include criterion IDs and awarded points so arithmetic is reproducible. Explain separately how confirmed defects and unverified evidence limited credit. This is not an overall usefulness grade.\\r\\n7. **Top improvements:** up to three ranked actions, each labeled repair, verify, or optional improvement, with evidence, exact next action, expected practical benefit, and what would prove it complete. Refer to finding IDs instead of repeating them in full.\\r\\n8. **Progress since the previous audit:** link the baseline, show finding transitions and evidence from [history.md](history.md), and explain any comparable score change. Separate actual repairs, newly verified evidence, regressions, and coverage/rubric changes. No prior comparable report means a new baseline, not an invented improvement.\\r\\n9. **Next run:** a ready-to-use `/level-up` prompt carrying the highest-value gap, evidence, and acceptance check; use `/link <target> <purpose>` for a routing-only fix. Recommend these commands only if installed; otherwise give the equivalent plain-language task. `/grill-me`, if available, is useful for genuinely missing context, not for facts already stored elsewhere.\\r\\n10. **Saved record:** write and read back the complete report using [templates/report.md](templates/report.md). Link the actual saved file in the final reply. The chat can be concise; the saved record must retain the score breakdown, evidence, compatibility checks, and carried finding ledger. Never say saved without confirming it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Required section: AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md findings\\r\\n\\r\\nStart by naming the exact root manuals inspected and the scoped manuals sampled. Mark each root manual **checked**, **missing**, or **not checked**, with a reason where needed. State whether their shared guidance agrees, conflicts, or intentionally differs. Do not imply that inspecting one file verifies the other; a setup with only one applicable manual does not need a duplicate merely to pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen show a compact findings table:\\r\\n\\r\\n| File and section/line | Rule or missing route | Finding and practical effect | Recommended change |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Exact clickable file reference | Short quote or precise paraphrase; label omissions explicitly | What was verified, stale, conflicting, broken, or unverified, and how that affects finding or using information | Specific edit or verification step |\\r\\n\\r\\nSeparate **problems in the manual itself** from **problems in files it routes to**. For example, a stale target index is a downstream maintenance gap unless the manual also directs readers to the wrong index. Name the downstream file and the route that reaches it. Include one brief statement about what the manuals already do well. If no issues are found, explicitly say \\"No operating-manual issues found in the inspected scope\\" and state any coverage limits; do not invent findings to fill the table.\\r\\n\\r\\nFinish the section with whether any manuals were changed. In a standard read-only audit, say **\\"No operating manuals were changed.\\"** These findings inform the existing Four-Cs criteria and caps; do not add a fifth score or double-count deductions. Apply this section to both chat reports and saved reports.\\r\\n\\r\\nRecommend rerunning `/audit` after the selected fix and weekly during active setup, then at a sensible maintenance interval. Compare with a previous saved report only when scope and rubric match; v1 scores need a new baseline. Scores can decrease when evidence becomes stale. Do not promise a higher score from another run alone.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe audit is read-only toward inspected systems: no repairs, file moves, installation, scheduler changes, messages, or external writes. Its sole default write is a new local audit report under `audits/` (create the directory if needed). Preserve earlier reports, redact secrets and unnecessary private records, and do not update business memory or source-of-truth files from audit observations. On an incomplete run, save a clearly marked partial report when possible; do not present it as a completed audit or fabricate a score. If saving fails, report that explicitly and return the report in chat.\\r\\n"}'}]

ment.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\distill.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\delight.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\critique.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\craft.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\colorize.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\color-and-contrast.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\cognitive-load.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\clarify.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\brand.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\bolder.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\audit.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\animate.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\impeccable\\\\reference\\\\adapt.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\notebooklm\\\\docs\\\\cli-reference.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\skill-creator\\\\references\\\\schemas.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\notebooklm\\\\tests\\\\unit\\\\test_chat_references.py\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\wiki-schema.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\tooling.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\agent-configs\\\\gemini.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\agent-configs\\\\cursor.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\agent-configs\\\\codex.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\second-brain\\\\references\\\\agent-configs\\\\claude-code.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\using-superpowers\\\\references\\\\gemini-tools.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\using-superpowers\\\\references\\\\copilot-tools.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\using-superpowers\\\\references\\\\codex-tools.md\\n.agents\\\\skills\\\\notebooklm\\\\tests\\\\cassettes\\\\chat_ask_with_references.yaml\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f23468","wall_time_seconds":0.3927871,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":6502,"output":"---\\r\\nname: impeccable\\r\\ndescription: Use when the user wants to design, redesign, shape, critique, audit, polish, clarify, distill, harden, optimize, adapt, animate, colorize, extract, or otherwise improve a frontend interface. Covers websites, landing pages, dashboards, product UI, app shells, components, forms, settings, onboarding, and empty states. Handles UX review, visual hierarchy, information architecture, cognitive load, accessibility, performance, responsive behavior, theming, anti-patterns, typography, fonts, spacing, layout, alignment, color, motion, micro-interactions, UX copy, error states, edge cases, i18n, and reusable design systems or tokens. Also use for bland designs that need to become bolder or more delightful, loud designs that should become quieter, live browser iteration on UI elements, or ambitious visual effects that should feel technically extraordinary. Not for backend-only or non-UI tasks.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nDesigns and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Setup (non-optional)\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Gate | Required check | If fail |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |\\r\\n| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user\'s original prompt alone. |\\r\\n| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |\\r\\n| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |\\r\\n| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |\\r\\n| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |\\r\\n\\r\\nCodex-style agents must state this before editing files:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nIMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nFor `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.\\r\\n\\r\\nOther harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Context gathering\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).\\r\\n\\r\\n- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.\\r\\n- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.\\r\\n\\r\\nLoad both in one call:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnode .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nConsume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output\'s `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the output is already in this session\'s conversation history, don\'t re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.\\r\\n\\r\\n`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you\'ve run `live.mjs`, don\'t also run `load-context.mjs` this session.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user\'s original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*\\"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output\\"*), then proceed.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Register\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).\\r\\n\\r\\nIdentify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself (\\"landing page\\" vs \\"dashboard\\"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its \\"Users\\" and \\"Product Purpose\\" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.\\r\\n\\r\\nLoad the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Shared design laws\\r\\n\\r\\nApply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don\'t hold back.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Color\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.\\r\\n- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).\\r\\n- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:\\r\\n  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.\\r\\n  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.\\r\\n  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.\\r\\n  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.\\r\\n- The \\"one accent ≤10%\\" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don\'t collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Theme\\r\\n\\r\\nDark vs. light is never a default. Not dark \\"because tools look cool dark.\\" Not light \\"to be safe.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn\'t force the answer, it\'s not concrete enough — add detail until it does.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Observability dashboard\\" does not force an answer. \\"SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room\\" does. Run the sentence, not the category.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Typography\\r\\n\\r\\n- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.\\r\\n- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.\\r\\n- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they\'re truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.\\r\\n- Don\'t wrap everything in a container. Most things don\'t need one.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Motion\\r\\n\\r\\n- Don\'t animate CSS layout properties.\\r\\n- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Absolute bans\\r\\n\\r\\nMatch-and-refuse. If you\'re about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.\\r\\n- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.\\r\\n- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.\\r\\n- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.\\r\\n- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.\\r\\n- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Copy\\r\\n\\r\\n- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.\\r\\n- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The AI slop test\\r\\n\\r\\nIf someone could look at this interface and say \\"AI made that\\" without doubt, it\'s failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — \\"observability → dark blue\\", \\"healthcare → white + teal\\", \\"finance → navy + gold\\", \\"crypto → neon on black\\" — it\'s the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn\'t obvious from the domain.\\r\\n- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — \\"AI workflow tool that\'s not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic\\", \\"fintech that\'s not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode\\" — it\'s the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn\'t. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register\'s [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n| Command | Category | Description | Reference |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |\\r\\n| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |\\r\\n| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |\\r\\n| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |\\r\\n| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |\\r\\n| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |\\r\\n| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |\\r\\n| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |\\r\\n| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |\\r\\n| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |\\r\\n| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |\\r\\n| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |\\r\\n| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |\\r\\n| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |\\r\\n| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |\\r\\n| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |\\r\\n| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |\\r\\n| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |\\r\\n| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |\\r\\n| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |\\r\\n| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |\\r\\n| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |\\r\\n| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |\\r\\n\\r\\nPlus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Routing rules\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they\'d like to do.\\r\\n2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.\\r\\n3. **First word doesn\'t match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.\\r\\n\\r\\nSetup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don\'t re-invoke `$impeccable`.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pin / Unpin\\r\\n\\r\\n**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnode .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nValid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script\'s result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.\\r\\nRun systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don\'t fix issues — document them for other commands to address.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what\'s measurable and verifiable in the implementation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Diagnostic Scan\\r\\n\\r\\nRun comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Accessibility (A11y)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check for**:\\r\\n- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)\\r\\n- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states\\r\\n- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps\\r\\n- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons\\r\\n- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions\\r\\n- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators\\r\\n\\r\\n**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Performance\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check for**:\\r\\n- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops\\r\\n- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames\\r\\n- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change\\r\\n- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies\\r\\n- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization\\r\\n\\r\\n**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Theming\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check for**:\\r\\n- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens\\r\\n- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme\\r\\n- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types\\r\\n- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don\'t update on theme change\\r\\n\\r\\n**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Responsive Design\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check for**:\\r\\n- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile\\r\\n- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px\\r\\n- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports\\r\\n- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases\\r\\n- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants\\r\\n\\r\\n**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck against ALL the **DON\'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Generate Report\\r\\n\\r\\n### Audit Health Score\\r\\n\\r\\n| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |\\r\\n|---|-----------|-------|-------------|\\r\\n| 1 | Accessibility | ? | [most critical a11y issue or \\"--\\"] |\\r\\n| 2 | Performance | ? | |\\r\\n| 3 | Responsive Design | ? | |\\r\\n| 4 | Theming | ? | |\\r\\n| 5 | Anti-Patterns | ? | |\\r\\n| **Total** | | **??/20** | **[Rating band]** |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Rating bands**: 18-20 Excellent (minor polish), 14-17 Good (address weak dimensions), 10-13 Acceptable (significant work needed), 6-9 Poor (major overhaul), 0-5 Critical (fundamental issues)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Anti-Patterns Verdict\\r\\n**Start here.** Pass/fail: Does this look AI-generated? List specific tells. Be brutally honest.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Executive Summary\\r\\n- Audit Health Score: **??/20** ([rating band])\\r\\n- Total issues found (count by severity: P0/P1/P2/P3)\\r\\n- Top 3-5 critical issues\\r\\n- Recommended next steps\\r\\n\\r\\n### Detailed Findings by Severity\\r\\n\\r\\nTag every issue with **P0-P3 severity**:\\r\\n- **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion — fix immediately\\r\\n- **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation — fix before release\\r\\n- **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists — fix in next pass\\r\\n- **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact — fix if time permits\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each issue, document:\\r\\n- **[P?] Issue name**\\r\\n- **Location**: Component, file, line\\r\\n- **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Anti-Pattern\\r\\n- **Impact**: How it affects users\\r\\n- **WCAG/Standard**: Which standard it violates (if applicable)\\r\\n- **Recommendation**: How to fix it\\r\\n- **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Patterns & Systemic Issues\\r\\n\\r\\nIdentify recurring problems that indicate systemic gaps rather than one-off mistakes:\\r\\n- \\"Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens\\"\\r\\n- \\"Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### Positive Findings\\r\\n\\r\\nNote what\'s working well — good practices to maintain and replicate.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Recommended Actions\\r\\n\\r\\nList recommended commands in priority order (P0 first, then P1, then P2):\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context from audit findings)\\r\\n2. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Rules**: Only recommend commands from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset. Map findings to the most appropriate command. End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter presenting the summary, tell the user:\\r\\n\\r\\n> You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see your score improve.\\r\\n\\r\\n**IMPORTANT**: Be thorough but actionable. Too many P3 issues creates noise. Focus on what actually matters.\\r\\n\\r\\n**NEVER**:\\r\\n- Report issues without explaining impact (why does this matter?)\\r\\n- Provide generic recommendations (be specific and actionable)\\r\\n- Skip positive findings (celebrate what works)\\r\\n- Forget to prioritize (everything can\'t be P0)\\r\\n- Report false positives without verification\\r\\n\\r\\nRemember: You\'re a technical quality auditor. Document systematically, prioritize ruthlessly, cite specific code locations, and provide clear paths to improvement.\\r\\n# UX Writing\\r\\n\\r\\n## The Button Label Problem\\r\\n\\r\\n**Never use \\"OK\\", \\"Submit\\", or \\"Yes/No\\".** These are lazy and ambiguous. Use specific verb + object patterns:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Bad | Good | Why |\\r\\n|-----|------|-----|\\r\\n| OK | Save changes | Says what will happen |\\r\\n| Submit | Create account | Outcome-focused |\\r\\n| Yes | Delete message | Confirms the action |\\r\\n| Cancel | Keep editing | Clarifies what \\"cancel\\" means |\\r\\n| Click here | Download PDF | Describes the destination |\\r\\n\\r\\n**For destructive actions**, name the destruction:\\r\\n- \\"Delete\\" not \\"Remove\\" (delete is permanent, remove implies recoverable)\\r\\n- \\"Delete 5 items\\" not \\"Delete selected\\" (show the count)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Error Messages: The Formula\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery error message should answer: (1) What happened? (2) Why? (3) How to fix it? Example: \\"Email address isn\'t valid. Please include an @ symbol.\\" not \\"Invalid input\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n### Error Message Templates\\r\\n\\r\\n| Situation | Template |\\r\\n|-----------|----------|\\r\\n| **Format error** | \\"[Field] needs to be [format]. Example: [example]\\" |\\r\\n| **Missing required** | \\"Please enter [what\'s missing]\\" |\\r\\n| **Permission denied** | \\"You don\'t have access to [thing]. [What to do instead]\\" |\\r\\n| **Network error** | \\"We couldn\'t reach [thing]. Check your connection and [action].\\" |\\r\\n| **Server error** | \\"Something went wrong on our end. We\'re looking into it. [Alternative action]\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Don\'t Blame the User\\r\\n\\r\\nReframe errors: \\"Please enter a date in MM/DD/YYYY format\\" not \\"You entered an invalid date\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n## Empty States Are Opportunities\\r\\n\\r\\nEmpty states are onboarding moments: (1) Acknowledge briefly, (2) Explain the value of filling it, (3) Provide a clear action. \\"No projects yet. Create your first one to get started.\\" not just \\"No items\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice vs Tone\\r\\n\\r\\n**Voice** is your brand\'s personality—consistent everywhere.\\r\\n**Tone** adapts to the moment.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Moment | Tone Shift |\\r\\n|--------|------------|\\r\\n| Success | Celebratory, brief: \\"Done! Your changes are live.\\" |\\r\\n| Error | Empathetic, helpful: \\"That didn\'t work. Here\'s what to try...\\" |\\r\\n| Loading | Reassuring: \\"Saving your work...\\" |\\r\\n| Destructive confirm | Serious, clear: \\"Delete this project? This can\'t be undone.\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Never use humor for errors.** Users are already frustrated. Be helpful, not cute.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Writing for Accessibility\\r\\n\\r\\n**Link text** must have standalone meaning—\\"View pricing plans\\" not \\"Click here\\". **Alt text** describes information, not the image—\\"Revenue increased 40% in Q4\\" not \\"Chart\\". Use `alt=\\"\\"` for decorative images. **Icon buttons** need `aria-label` for screen reader context.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Writing for Translation\\r\\n\\r\\n### Plan for Expansion\\r\\n\\r\\nGerman text is ~30% longer than English. Allocate space:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Language | Expansion |\\r\\n|----------|-----------|\\r\\n| German | +30% |\\r\\n| French | +20% |\\r\\n| Finnish | +30-40% |\\r\\n| Chinese | -30% (fewer chars, but same width) |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Translation-Friendly Patterns\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep numbers separate (\\"New messages: 3\\" not \\"You have 3 new messages\\"). Use full sentences as single strings (word order varies by language). Avoid abbreviations (\\"5 minutes ago\\" not \\"5 mins ago\\"). Give translators context about where strings appear.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Consistency: The Terminology Problem\\r\\n\\r\\nPick one term and stick with it:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Inconsistent | Consistent |\\r\\n|--------------|------------|\\r\\n| Delete / Remove / Trash | Delete |\\r\\n| Settings / Preferences / Options | Settings |\\r\\n| Sign in / Log in / Enter | Sign in |\\r\\n| Create / Add / New | Create |\\r\\n\\r\\nBuild a terminology glossary and enforce it. Variety creates confusion.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Avoid Redundant Copy\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the heading explains it, the intro is redundant. If the button is clear, don\'t explain it again. Say it once, say it well.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Loading States\\r\\n\\r\\nBe specific: \\"Saving your draft...\\" not \\"Loading...\\". For long waits, set expectations (\\"This usually takes 30 seconds\\") or show progress.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Confirmation Dialogs: Use Sparingly\\r\\n\\r\\nMost confirmation dialogs are design failures—consider undo instead. When you must confirm: name the action, explain consequences, use specific button labels (\\"Delete project\\" / \\"Keep project\\", not \\"Yes\\" / \\"No\\").\\r\\n\\r\\n## Form Instructions\\r\\n\\r\\nShow format with placeholders, not instructions. For non-obvious fields, explain why you\'re asking.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n**Avoid**: Jargon without explanation. Blaming users (\\"You made an error\\" → \\"This field is required\\"). Vague errors (\\"Something went wrong\\"). Varying terminology for variety. Humor for errors.\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"7fb3ff","wall_time_seconds":0.9504891,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":8277,"output":"{\\n  \\"hasProduct\\": true,\\n  \\"product\\": \\"# Product\\\\n\\\\n<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->\\\\n\\\\n## Scope\\\\n\\\\nApp-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.\\\\n\\\\nA rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface\'s brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.\\\\n\\\\nThe repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.\\\\n\\\\n## Platform\\\\n\\\\nweb\\\\n\\\\n## Users\\\\n\\\\n**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.\\\\n\\\\n**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.\\\\n\\\\n**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.\\\\n\\\\n## Product Purpose\\\\n\\\\nLSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua\'s coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student\'s work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.\\\\n\\\\nFor a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.\\\\n\\\\nFor Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.\\\\n\\\\nSuccess is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.\\\\n\\\\n## Positioning\\\\n\\\\n**A real coach is in it.**\\\\n\\\\nThe claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student\'s work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.\\\\n\\\\n## Operating Context\\\\n\\\\nThe platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.\\\\n\\\\n- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.\\\\n- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).\\\\n- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student\'s effort.\\\\n- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.\\\\n- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.\\\\n\\\\n## Capabilities and Constraints\\\\n\\\\nConfirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teacher workspace.\\\\n\\\\nTechnical constraints:\\\\n\\\\n- React, TypeScript, Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui and Radix primitives, React Router, TanStack Query, Supabase.\\\\n- One motion library (`framer-motion`). One primary icon library (`lucide-react`). New work composes existing primitives before introducing a new dependency.\\\\n- Deployment is not a side effect of pushing to `main`. Production at lsatprep.study requires an explicit publish step.\\\\n- Database migrations in `supabase/migrations/` are an archive, not an applied ledger. Production schema changes go through their own path, and UI must not assume a table exists because a migration file does.\\\\n\\\\nTerminology is not defined here. It is defined per bounded context and mapped by `CONTEXT-MAP.md`. A word can carry different weight in different contexts, and collapsing them has already caused real bugs (a **flag** is not a **wrong answer**; **Set** and **Position** were separated for this reason).\\\\n\\\\n## Brand Commitments\\\\n\\\\n- **Name:** LSAT U, operating under Aspiring Attorneys. Production domain lsatprep.study.\\\\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect reads.** Joshua does not type them. A stray em-dash reads as machine-written and costs trust. This binds computed copy, utility copy, and authored copy alike.\\\\n- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a \\\\\\"coach voice, not industry voice\\\\\\" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua\'s decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.\\\\n\\\\n## Evidence on Hand\\\\n\\\\nReal:\\\\n\\\\n- A live production deployment at lsatprep.study with real enrolled students.\\\\n- Authored LSAT curriculum: bootcamp modules, guided drill banks, RC passages with per-question explanations, and practice test content.\\\\n- Real student work in the database: attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers, assignments.\\\\n- A real named instructor with a real coaching practice behind the product.\\\\n\\\\nAbsent, and never to be fabricated:\\\\n\\\\n- No testimonials, case studies, press coverage, score-improvement statistics, or named customer references exist in this repo. Do not write them into any surface.\\\\n- No pricing, licensing, or availability claims are established here.\\\\n- No third-party or copyright framing anywhere in student-facing copy.\\\\n\\\\n## Product Principles\\\\n\\\\n1. **Truthfulness over performance.** Every number, badge, streak, status, and progress claim traces to real stored data. An honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops. This is the one principle carried forward unchanged from the previous record, and it is the hardest of the five.\\\\n\\\\n2. **Compute the next step; do not present a grid.** A student arriving at a surface should be told what to do, not shown a menu and left to choose. A card grid is what a product offers when it does not know the student.\\\\n\\\\n3. **Attempt before explanation.** The student commits to an answer before the product explains anything. Passive reading does not build the skill. Reveal copy addresses what the student actually chose, not a generic key.\\\\n\\\\n4. **A measurement is never a gate.** Drills and checks measure after teaching. A miss is framed as the next thing to work on, not a grade or a failure. Nothing in the product rewards attendance rather than work.\\\\n\\\\n5. **The coach is not simulated.** Where the product speaks for Joshua, it is because Joshua said it. Where he has not, the product does not fill the silence with something that sounds like him. This is the operational form of the positioning claim.\\\\n\\\\n## Accessibility & Inclusion\\\\n\\\\n**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.\\\\n\\\\n- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.\\\\n- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.\\\\n- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.\\\\n- Touch targets are 44x44px, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text. This one is a house standard above the floor, not part of it: 44x44 is WCAG 2.5.5, which is Level AAA. The binding target-size rule is 2.5.8 at 24x24, so a control that clears 24x24 on a fine pointer clears the floor and is not a defect.\\\\n- A dense row of marks that exists to be read at a glance is the one place the 44x44 house standard yields. The Report Card\'s ledger is 22 to 26 ticks inside an 820px card; at 44px wide they would need 968 to 1144px, so meeting the standard means scrolling or wrapping, and the ledger stops being a single picture of the run. They are 44 tall and 28 to 30 wide, they clear 2.5.8, and that is the settled answer rather than an open defect. Joshua\'s call, 2026-09-09.\\\\n- Where a control has to stay small because it sits in chrome set in 11 or 13px type, the 44x44 target may be an explicit transparent overlay centred on the control instead of the control\'s own box. An overlay is a target somebody designed and it is proved by hit-testing the four edges; wrapped text is an accident and still does not count. The runner\'s rail controls, the door\'s crumb out and the demonstration\'s Play button are all built this way.\\\\n- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.\\\\n- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.\\\\n\\",\\n  \\"productPath\\": \\"PRODUCT.md\\",\\n  \\"hasDesign\\": true,\\n  \\"design\\": workroom-page: \\\\\\"#fcfcfc\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-card: \\\\\\"#ffffff\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-raised: \\\\\\"#f7f7f7\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-fill: \\\\\\"#f2f2f2\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-ink: workroom-night-raised: \\\\\\"#1a1a1a\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-night-ink: \\\\\\"#fafafa\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-night-ink-muted: \\\\\\"#a3a3a3\\\\\\"\\\\n  workroom-night-hairline: \\\\\\"#262626\\\\\\"\\\\n  # ── Meaning (identical in both themes; semantic only) ──\\\\n  state-correct: \\\\\\"#10b77f\\\\\\"\\\\n  state-incorrect: \\\\\\"#f43e5c\\\\\\"\\\\n  state-warn:
