(theme-independent, light ground only) ──\\\\n  teacher-accent: \\\\\\"#c7841f\\\\\\"\\\\n  teacher-accent-hover: \\\\\\"#af7318\\\\\\"\\\\n  teacher-accent-strong: \\\\\\"#895910\\\\\\"\\\\n  teacher-accent-soft: \\\\\\"#e0a648\\\\\\"\\\\ntypography:\\\\n  body:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Lexend, Outfit, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontSize: \\\\\\"14px\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 400\\\\n    lineHeight: 1.6\\\\n    fontFeature: \\\\\\"\'rlig\' 1, \'calt\' 1, \'cv11\' 1\\\\\\"\\\\n  title:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Lexend, Outfit, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontSize: \\\\\\"24px\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 600\\\\n    lineHeight: 1\\\\n    letterSpacing: \\\\\\"-0.025em\\\\\\"\\\\n  label:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Lexend, Outfit, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontSize: \\\\\\"14px\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 500\\\\n    lineHeight: 1.4\\\\n  question:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Outfit, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontSize: \\\\\\"16px\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 400\\\\n    lineHeight: 1.6\\\\n  hall-display:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Fraunces, \'Iowan Old Style\', \'Times New Roman\', serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 380\\\\n    lineHeight: 1.1\\\\n    letterSpacing: \\\\\\"-0.015em\\\\\\"\\\\n  hall-inscription:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"Cinzel, \'Trajan Pro\', \'Times New Roman\', serif\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 500\\\\n    letterSpacing: \\\\\\"0.05em\\\\\\"\\\\n  hall-mono:\\\\n    fontFamily: \\\\\\"\'IBM Plex Mono\', ui-monospace, \'SF Mono\', monospace\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontSize: \\\\\\"13px\\\\\\"\\\\n    fontWeight: 400\\\\n    letterSpacing: \\\\\\"0.04em\\\\\\"\\\\nrounded:\\\\n  sm: \\\\\\"4px\\\\\\"\\\\n  md: \\\\\\"6px\\\\\\"\\\\n  lg: \\\\\\"8px\\\\\\"\\\\n  hall: \\\\\\"2px\\\\\\"\\\\n  pill: \\\\\\"9999px\\\\\\"\\\\nspacing:\\\\n  xs: \\\\\\"4px\\\\\\"\\\\n  sm: \\\\\\"8px\\\\\\"\\\\n  md: \\\\\\"16px\\\\\\"\\\\n  lg: \\\\\\"24px\\\\\\"\\\\n  xl: \\\\\\"32px\\\\\\"\\\\ncomponents:\\\\n  button-primary:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-page}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.md}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"8px 16px\\\\\\"\\\\n    height: \\\\\\"44px\\\\\\"\\\\n  button-primary-hover:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-page}\\\\\\"\\\\n  button-outline:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-page}\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.md}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"8px 16px\\\\\\"\\\\n    height: \\\\\\"44px\\\\\\"\\\\n  button-ghost:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"transparent\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.md}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"8px 16px\\\\\\"\\\\n    height: \\\\\\"44px\\\\\\"\\\\n  card:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-card}\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.lg}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"24px\\\\\\"\\\\n  input:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-page}\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.workroom-ink}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.md}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"8px 12px\\\\\\"\\\\n    height: \\\\\\"44px\\\\\\"\\\\n  hall-cta:\\\\n    backgroundColor: \\\\\\"transparent\\\\\\"\\\\n    textColor: \\\\\\"{colors.hall-marble}\\\\\\"\\\\n    rounded: \\\\\\"{rounded.hall}\\\\\\"\\\\n    padding: \\\\\\"0 38px\\\\\\"\\\\n    height: \\\\\\"60px\\\\\\"\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Design System: LSAT Academy\\\\n\\\\n## Overview\\\\n\\\\n**Creative North Star: \\\\\\"LSAT Academy\\\\\\"**\\\\n\\\\nOne institution with two registers, and the seam between them is deliberate.\\\\n\\\\n**The Hall** is the door. Unauthenticated, dark, and material: near-black ground, warm marble text, a bronze that carries the light, and a serif that behaves like carved lettering. Its own source calls it a museum nocturne, carved marble filmed in darkness, read by modern instruments. Its job is to make a prospective student feel they are entering somewhere, not signing up for something. It is the only place in the product where atmosphere outranks throughput.\\\\n\\\\n**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom\'s white they collapse. The Hall\'s bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom\'s page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.\\\\n\\\\nWhat they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.\\\\n\\\\nInside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room\'s paint.\\\\n\\\\n**Key Characteristics:**\\\\n- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.\\\\n- Colour means something or it is absent.\\\\n- Flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows.\\\\n- Precise and quiet controls that stay out of the reader\'s way.\\\\n- Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are.\\\\n\\\\n## Colors\\\\n\\\\nTwo grounds, one shared vocabulary of meaning.\\\\n\\\\n### Primary\\\\n\\\\n- **Workroom Ink** (`#121212`): the Workroom\'s near-black. It is the text colour, the fill of every primary button, and the colour of the focus ring. The Workroom has no accent, so ink carries emphasis by itself.\\\\n- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall\'s warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall\'s night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.\\\\n\\\\n### Secondary\\\\n\\\\n- **Hall Cyan** (`#8ad8e2`): the Hall\'s cool instrument note against the bronze, and the colour of its focus outlines. Rare by design; it marks precision where bronze marks warmth.\\\\n- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.\\\\n\\\\n### Tertiary\\\\n\\\\nIndividual rooms carry their own palettes behind a wrapper class and are documented in their own surface briefs, not here. The wrappers in use are `.classroom-root`, `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk`, and the eight Activity Centre worlds.\\\\n\\\\n`.classroom-root` was added to this list on 2026-09-08, after an audit found the classroom running on Outfit and a warm palette while this record still described it as neutral Workroom chrome. It is a sanctioned world, not drift: the warmth and the typeface are deliberate and they are what makes the classroom feel like a study rather than a dashboard. The record was wrong, not the code. Its brief is `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md`. Note that this is the one sanctioned world that is also a corridor, so the rule below about corridors never being worlds now has exactly one documented exception.\\\\n\\\\n### Neutral\\\\n\\\\n- **Workroom Page** (`#fcfcfc`) and **Card** (`#ffffff`): the page ground and the surface that sits on it. One step of separation, no more.\\\\n- **Workroom Raised** (`#f7f7f7`) and **Fill** (`#f2f2f2`): the two tonal steps available for layering and for resting fills.\\\\n- **Workroom Ink Muted** (`#737373`): secondary text. Measures 4.62:1 on the page, which clears the floor with almost nothing to spare. Do not lighten it.\\\\n- **Workroom Hairline** (`#e0e0e0`) and **Field** (`#e6e6e6`): the borders that do the work shadows used to.\\\\n- **Hall Marble** (`#eae5da`), with secondary (`#a6a093`) and muted (`#6e695e`) steps: warm off-white text on the night ground.\\\\n\\\\n### State (semantic only, never decorative)\\\\n\\\\n- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student\'s attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.\\\\n- **Warn** (`#f59f0a`): a bonus, a stretch, a non-blocking notice.\\\\n- **Success** (`#21c45d`), **Warning** (`#f59f0a`), **Destructive** (`#dc2828`): system status, not student performance.\\\\n\\\\n### Named Rules\\\\n\\\\n**The Ground Decides Rule.** A warm colour is legible on the Hall\'s night and illegible on the Workroom\'s page. Never carry a colour across the seam on the strength of liking it; measure it against the ground it will land on first.\\\\n\\\\n**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.\\\\n\\\\n**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.\\\\n\\\\n## Typography\\\\n\\\\n**Workroom Body:** Lexend (with Outfit, then the system stack)\\\\n**Hall Display:** Fraunces (with Iowan Old Style, Times New Roman, serif)\\\\n**Hall Inscription:** Cinzel (with Trajan Pro, serif)\\\\n**Mono:** IBM Plex Mono\\\\n**Question Lock:** Outfit, held deliberately for LSAT question surfaces\\\\n\\\\n**Character:** The Workroom is one humanist sans doing everything, sized and weighted rather than swapped. The Hall is a serif institution: an optical serif for its headings and an inscriptional face for its few carved lines. The split is the whole typographic idea. Inside, type gets out of the way; at the door, type is the architecture.\\\\n\\\\n### Hierarchy\\\\n\\\\n- **Title** (600, 24px, line-height 1, tracking -0.025em): card and section headings in the Workroom.\\\\n- **Body** (400, 14px rising to 16px at 640px and above, line-height 1.6): everything a student reads in the Workroom chrome.\\\\n- **Question** (400, 16px, line-height 1.6, Outfit): LSAT stimulus and answer choices. Font-locked to Outfit even though the rest of the app moved to Lexend, so question text does not shift when the shell\'s face changes.\\\\n- **Label** (500, 14px): button and control labels.\\\\n- **Hall Display** (380, fluid, tracking -0.015em, Fraunces): Hall headings. The unusually light 380 weight is the point; it reads as carved rather than printed.\\\\n- **Hall Inscription** (500, tracking 0.05em, Cinzel): the Hall\'s few short carved lines. Never body copy.\\\\n- **Hall Mono** (400, 13px, tracking 0.04em, IBM Plex Mono): the Hall\'s instrument labels.\\\\n\\\\n### Named Rules\\\\n\\\\n**The One Face Rule.** The Workroom uses a single family. Hierarchy is built from size, weight and space. A second family inside the Workroom is a defect unless it is the Outfit question lock.\\\\n\\\\n**The Question Never Moves Rule.** LSAT question text is font-locked. A change to the shell\'s typeface must not reflow a passage a student is mid-way through reading.\\\\n\\\\n**The Serif Stays At The Door Rule.** Fraunces and Cinzel belong to the Hall. A serif appearing in the Workroom chrome means the seam has leaked.\\\\n\\\\n## Layout\\\\n\\\\nThe Workroom is a centred single column with generous side gutters. The container centres by default, carries 32px of horizontal padding, and stops widening at 1400px.\\\\n\\\\nBreakpoints are the framework defaults up to 1280px, with the largest step pulled in to 1400px. Body type steps from 14px to 16px at 640px, and form controls are forced to 16px below 768px so that iOS does not zoom the page when a field takes focus. Named breakpoints `phone` (up to 600px), `tablet` (601 to 900px) and `desktop` (901px and up) exist for bootcamp surfaces.\\\\n\\\\nSpacing runs on a 4px base. The steps in real use are 4, 8, 16, 24 and 32. Card padding is 24px on all sides; button padding is 8px vertical and 16px horizontal.\\\\n\\\\nReading measure matters more here than in most products. Passage and stimulus text is held to a comfortable line length rather than allowed to run the full container width.\\\\n\\\\n### Named Rules\\\\n\\\\n**The Reading Measure Rule.** Any surface carrying an LSAT passage, stimulus, or explanation constrains its own line length. The container\'s maximum is a ceiling for layout, never a licence for prose.\\\\n\\\\n## Elevation & Depth\\\\n\\\\n**Flat, with hairline separation.** Surfaces sit on the page rather than above it. A card is distinguished from its ground by a one-pixel border and, where more is needed, by one step of background tone. There are no drop shadows in the Workroom.\\\\n\\\\nThis is a decision, not a description. The codebase currently ships five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground, from `0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` up to `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`, and applies the smallest of them to every card. Because the app ships light by default, those shadows land at 40 percent black under a white card. The light theme never redefined them, and the one glow token is white, so it is invisible on white. The tokens are retired by this record and the code has not caught up yet.\\\\n\\\\nThe Hall is the exception and keeps its own depth: fixed ambience gradients, a grain overlay, and an inset highlight on its call to action. That depth is atmospheric rather than structural, and it does not transfer.\\\\n\\\\n### Named Rules\\\\n\\\\n**The Flat Rule.** Depth in the Workroom comes from a hairline or a tonal step. A `box-shadow` on a Workroom surface is a defect.\\\\n\\\\n**The Two-Step Rule.** Page, then card, then at most one raised step. A surface that needs a fourth level of layering is a surface that needs restructuring.\\\\n\\\\n## Shapes\\\\n\\\\nCorners are gently rounded and consistent: 8px for cards and containers, 6px for buttons and inputs, 4px for the smallest chips and insets, and a full pill only for genuine badges. The scale derives from one 0.5rem radius token, so a change to the base moves the whole system together.\\\\n\\\\nThe Hall uses a near-square 2px radius instead. Its edges are cut, not softened, which is what separates a carved surface from a card.\\\\n\\\\nBorders are one pixel. There is no second border weight in the Workroom.\\\\n\\\\n### Named Rules\\\\n\\\\n**The Hairline Rule.** One pixel, always. A thicker border used as a coloured accent stripe on the leading edge of a surface is banned, including when faked with an inset shadow. Active state is carried by background tone.\\\\n\\\\n## Components\\\\n\\\\n### Buttons\\\\n\\\\n- **Character:** precise and quiet. A button is a plain rectangle with a label. It darkens slightly on hover and does nothing else. It does not lift, does not cast a shadow, and does not scale on press.\\\\n- **Shape:** gently rounded (6px).\\\\n- **Primary:** ink fill (`#121212`), page-coloured label, 8px by 16px padding.\\\\n- **Outline:** page background, one-pixel field border, ink label.\\\\n- **Ghost:** no fill at rest, a tonal fill on hover.\\\\n- **Sizes:** default 44px tall, small 40px, large 48px, icon 44 by 44. Every size clears the 44px touch minimum by being that height, never by relying on wrapped text or padding accidents.\\\\n- **Focus:** a solid two-pixel ink outline, offset two pixels from the edge. This is the only focus treatment in the Workroom.\\\\n- **Hall CTA:** 60px tall, near-square corners, transparent fill over a bronze hairline, with a bronze sheen that sweeps across on hover and a cyan outline on focus.\\\\n\\\\n### Cards / Containers\\\\n\\\\n- **Corner:** 8px.\\\\n- **Background:** card white on the page ground.\\\\n- **Border:** one-pixel hairline. This is what separates the card, not a shadow.\\\\n- **Shadow:** none.\\\\n- **Padding:** 24px, with header, content and footer sharing that inset.\\\\n\\\\n### Inputs / Fields\\\\n\\\\n- **Style:** page background, one-pixel field border, 6px corners, 44px minimum height, 8px by 12px padding.\\\\n- **Focus:** the same two-pixel ink outline as buttons, offset two pixels. Do not express focus as a border colour change alone; a border shift is too quiet to serve as the only indicator.\\\\n- **Mobile:** 16px text below 768px, so focusing a field never zooms the viewport.\\\\n\\\\n### Navigation\\\\n\\\\nNeutral chrome throughout. The sidebar runs one tonal step off the page ground with a hairline edge, and the active item is marked by background tone rather than by a coloured rule on its leading edge.\\\\n\\\\n### Signature Component: the scoped world\\\\n\\\\nThe product\'s distinctive pattern is not a control, it is a container. A room commits fully to its own palette, typefaces and atmosphere behind a single wrapper class, and nothing inside it leaks out. Scene layers are `aria-hidden` furniture painted beneath the content, so the accessible tree is identical whether the room is painted or bare. A new world is added by writing a new wrapper and its prefixed tokens, never by adding hex to a shared component.\\\\n\\\\n## Do\'s and Don\'ts\\\\n\\\\n### Do:\\\\n\\\\n- **Do** resolve every colour from a token, and treat a token that resolves to nothing as a defect. Verify a new token renders before documenting it.\\\\n- **Do** give every interactive element a visible focus indicator: a solid two-pixel ink outline offset two pixels in the Workroom, cyan in the Hall.\\\\n- **Do** check contrast against the ground the colour will actually land on, in the theme that actually ships, in a browser. The theme file has read as correct while the page rendered a light card on a near-black ground.\\\\n- **Do** separate surfaces with a one-pixel hairline and one tonal step.\\\\n- **Do** keep colour semantic: correct, incorrect, warning, danger. Nothing else earns it.\\\\n- **Do** scope a new room\'s identity behind its own wrapper class with prefixed tokens, the way the bootcamps and the eight Activity worlds already do.\\\\n- **Do** hold LSAT passage and stimulus text to a readable line length, and leave it on the Outfit question lock.\\\\n- **Do** end every world\'s stylesheet with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that stops continuous motion rather than speeding it up.\\\\n\\\\n### Don\'t:\\\\n\\\\n- **Don\'t** add a `box-shadow` to a Workroom surface. Depth is hairline and tone.\\\\n- **Don\'t** carry a Hall colour into the Workroom. Bronze reads 8.58:1 on the Hall\'s night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom\'s page.\\\\n- **Don\'t** introduce a warm or metallic accent into the Workroom to make it feel premium. Premium here is depth, hierarchy and type.\\\\n- **Don\'t** remove the browser\'s focus outline without shipping a replacement in the same rule. `outline-none` with no working ring is worse than leaving the default alone.\\\\n- **Don\'t** animate the focus indicator. A `transition-all` on a control means the ring fades in, which delays the one signal a keyboard user depends on.\\\\n- **Don\'t** use a border thicker than one pixel as a coloured accent stripe, including when faked with an inset shadow.\\\\n- **Don\'t** put a second typeface into the Workroom chrome, and don\'t let a serif cross in from the Hall.\\\\n- **Don\'t** animate layout properties. Animate `opacity`, `transform` and `color`. No bounce, no elastic, no lift on hover.\\\\n- **Don\'t** write a hex value into a component. If a room needs its own colour, promote it to named tokens behind that room\'s wrapper.\\\\n\\\\n<!--\\\\nKnown gaps between this record and the code, as of 2026-09-05. Recorded rather\\\\nthan hidden; none of them were fixed by writing this file.\\\\n\\\\n1. FIXED 2026-09-05. The shared Button and Input had no visible focus indicator:\\\\n   `focus-visible:outline-none` worked, `focus-visible:ring-3` generated no CSS\\\\n   (Tailwind 3 has no `ring-3`), and `focus-visible:ring-accent-bronze/50` pointed at\\\\n   an undefined variable. Both now use `outline-2 outline-offset-2 outline-foreground`,\\\\n   and `transition-all` was narrowed so the ring no longer fades in. A zero-specificity\\\\n   `:where(...):focus-visible` floor in `src/index.css` catches any control that\\\\n   defines nothing, while still losing to the Hall\'s cyan and the scoped bootcamp rings.\\\\n   Verified in a browser on all five Button variants and the Input: 18.26:1 in light,\\\\n   18.97:1 in the dark alternate, against the 3:1 an indicator needs.\\\\n\\\\n2. FIXED 2026-09-05. `--accent-bronze`, `--accent-warm` and `--glow-bronze` were\\\\n   consumed by tailwind.config.ts and defined in no commit in the repo\'s history.\\\\n   They arrived 2026-04-06 in \\\\\\"Imported LSATATTORNEYS project files\\\\\\". `#b06a3e` was\\\\n   inferred from a literal rgba in the `glow-sm` shadow on 2026-06-26 and written into\\\\n   the previous DESIGN.md as a brand accent it never was. All three names and the\\\\n   bronze-literal `glow-sm` shadow are removed, and their thirteen call sites now use\\\\n   neutral tokens. Three of those call sites were visibly broken, not merely untokenized:\\\\n   the journal ReviewForm\'s Next button rendered near-white text on a transparent\\\\n   background, its active step dot was invisible while the inactive dots showed, and the\\\\n   Annotate toggle\'s active state was indistinguishable from its inactive state.\\\\n   `docs/chart-palette.md` and `scripts/validate-chart-palette.mjs` still carry the\\\\n   inherited hex and have not been touched.\\\\n\\\\n3. Shadow tokens are dark-tuned and the app ships light. `shadow-sm` puts\\\\n   rgba(0,0,0,0.4) under a white card. Retired above; still applied by `ui/card.tsx`.\\\\n\\\\n4. Contrast failures in the shipped light theme, measured: `--text-tertiary` 3.28:1,\\\\n   `--success` 2.24:1, `--warning` 2.08:1, `--state-correct` 2.53:1,\\\\n   `--state-incorrect` 3.60:1, `--teacher-accent` 3.03:1 as text. In the dark\\\\n   alternate, `--text-tertiary` 4.18:1. AA needs 4.5:1 for normal text.\\\\n\\\\n5. Components are tactile today, not quiet. Buttons lift, scale on press, and gain\\\\n   a shadow on hover; cards carry `shadow-sm`. The record above chooses quiet and flat.\\\\n-->\\\\n\\",\\n  \\"designPath\\": \\"DESIGN.md\\",\\n  \\"migrated\\": false,\\n  \\"contextDir\\": \\"C:\\\\\\\\Users\\\\\\\\Joshu\\\\\\\\dev\\\\\\\\ai-os-projects\\\\\\\\lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\\"\\n}\\n"}'}]

previous : [...previous, questions[index].id]);\\r\\n103:   }\\r\\n104:   function next() {\\r\\n105:     if (current + 1 < questions.length) return move(current + 1);\\r\\n106:     if (questions.length === baselineQuestions.length) {\\r\\n107:       const errors = [...new Set(baselineQuestions.flatMap(item => results[item.id]?.errors ?? []))];\\r\\n108:       const confirmations: Question[] = errors.flatMap(error => {\\r\\n109:         const items = error === \'equivalence\' && results[\'mixed-transfer-4\']?.errors.includes(\'equivalence\') ? [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e65d3d","wall_time_seconds":2.1357936,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1079,"output":"grounded in a concrete set of ten cookies before drawing conclusions from the words.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe cut line is vague and does not sound like Joshua teaching. It makes the reader\\r\\ndecode “fits a description” before learning any of the three words. It also risks\\r\\nblurring the difference between a count that is possible and one that is guaranteed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** open with the actual distinction or definition Joshua taught. Use a\\r\\nconcrete example to show what each statement proves, then introduce notation and\\r\\nthe inferences. Do not write an abstract umbrella sentence merely to announce the\\r\\ntopic.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference quality does not authorize reference topics (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua requested the Some, Most, and All note as a teaching-flow and visual-quality reference. Quantity examples and exercises do not belong in the separate facts/rules note. Remove that off-topic material; retain the distinction between what a rule says and what a fact establishes. Before future recaps, read the accumulated corrections in the Post Session Recap skill\'s `references/recap-calibration.md`. Preserve source-driven scope while matching the reference\'s teaching quality.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Conditional practice: explain the decision instead of naming the operation (2026-10-07)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected “same rule, different words,” “equivalent-rule comparison,” “find the requirement, then place it,” “work through the expressions below,” “first choose the complete target,” “read the whole idea,” and “sentence X of Y” when these stood in for a useful explanation or made the drill feel like a collection of labeled sections. “Complete target” and similar terminology should not carry a beginner\'s instructions without a concrete meaning attached.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe revised local copy asks what the learner needs to decide: “Who does the rule say will receive a wristband?”, “What kind of key is needed to open the cabinet?”, and “What kind of pass does entry require?” The lesson demonstrates why before noon, current, registered and with yellow tags matter. Dropping one of those words changes which cases the rule covers. An arrow is checked by imagining its left condition and asking what the sentence then establishes, rather than treating the first English clause as the left side.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua also asked for the mixed drill to train a skill and build a habit, with one question visible and a progress bar. The current local response uses five stages with a concrete purpose for each: retain the full conditions, build a diagram, attempt a translation before supplied labels, compare meanings, then transfer to unfamiliar cases. Error feedback distinguishes dropped qualifications, reversed roles, required versus enough and changed meaning. A fresh question checks the repaired distinction; correcting the exposed answer alone does not demonstrate transfer. Written translations are compared by the learner and are not represented as automatically graded.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep modality in the examples. An electric vehicle that is denied permission disproves the claim that being electric guarantees permission; a vehicle merely staying outside does not. A required action is an obligation, not a fact proving compliance. Confirmation practice for that mistake must test another obligation rather than an unrelated wording comparison.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are demonstrated corrections and the current implementation response, not approval of every exact replacement sentence or proof that the voice matches Joshua identically. The [selected voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still governs wording, cadence, concrete reasoning and scope. [Current session record](log.md) distinguishes local work from publication.\\r\\n\\r\\nFurther steering in the same session: “Keep the complete condition” was itself awkward as a visible heading. Ask who or what the particular rule covers instead. Joshua also rejected the landing page\'s total question/stage counts, per-stage counts, long stage list and padded explanation of repairs. A shorter launch should explain the immediate task; each stage should introduce its purpose and instructions when the learner reaches it. That latest flow revision was requested while final verification was still pending, so this record does not certify its implementation.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

The families alternate and no label is shown until the\\r\\n// learner has checked a translation. Do not promise an unpredictable order.\\r\\nexport const mixedFamilyItems: MixedItem[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    sentence: \'The match moves indoors if the forecast shows lightning.\', word: \'if\', family: \'sufficient\',\\r\\n    target: \'the forecast shows lightning\', partial: \'the forecast\', other: \'the match moves indoors\',\\r\\n    left: \'Forecast shows lightning\', right: \'Match moves indoors\',\\r\\n    partialFeedback: \\"The forecast alone isn\'t something that can be true or false. If refers to the whole clause: the forecast shows lightning.\\",\\r\\n    otherFeedback: \'Moving indoors is what the rule establishes. The requirement is the signature: a parent signs the consent form.\',\\r\\n    otherFeedback: \'Joining the trip is the condition that carries the requirement. Only if points to what joining needs: a signed form.\',\\r\\n    reversed: \\"Your arrow promises a place on the trip to every student with a signed form. A student with a signed form who is ill on the day fits the sentence, so the signature isn\'t enough.\\",\\r\\n    why: \'Only if names a requirement. Any student on the trip has a signed form. A signed form leaves the trip open.\',\\r\\n    readback: \'If a student joins the choir trip, a parent has signed the consent form.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    sentence: \'Every bus leaving the depot after midnight stops at the hospital.\', word: \'every\', family: \'sufficient\',\\r\\n    target: \'bus leaving the depot after midnight\', partial: \'bus\', other: \'stops at the hospital\',\\r\\n    left: \'Bus leaves depot after midnight\', right: \'Stops at hospital\',\\r\\n    partialFeedback: \'Bus alone would cover the daytime buses too. Every refers to buses leaving the depot after midnight, so keep the depot and the time.\',\\r\\n    otherFeedback: \'Stopping at the hospital is what the rule establishes. Every points to the group it covers: buses leaving the depot after midnight.\',\\r\\n    reversed: \\"Your arrow says any bus at the hospital left the depot after midnight. A morning bus stopping there fits the sentence, so the stop doesn\'t settle when the bus left the depot.\\",\\r\\n    why: \'Every covers each bus leaving the depot after midnight. Pick one, and the rule tells you it stops at the hospital.\',\\r\\n    readback: \'If a bus leaves the depot after midnight, it stops at the hospital.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    sentence: \'A booked table is required to eat on the terrace.\', word: \'required\', family: \'necessary\',\\r\\n    target: \'a booked table\', partial: \'a table\', other: \'eat on the terrace\',\\r\\n    left: \'Eats on terrace\', right: \'Has booked table\',\\r\\n    partialFeedback: \\"A table drops booked, and booked is the requirement. The sentence doesn\'t ask for just any table; it asks for a booked one.\\",\\r\\n    otherFeedback: \'Eating on the terrace is what needs the booking. Required refers back to the idea before it: a booked table.\',\\r\\n    reversed: \'Your arrow sends everyone with a booking to the terrace. A diner could book a table and eat inside, and the sentence allows that.\',\\r\\n    why: \\"Here the target comes before required. Eating on the terrace needs a booked table. Phones placed in the same-day repair queue.\',\\r\\n    reversed: \'Your arrow puts every phone that arrived with a cracked screen in the same-day repair queue. A phone that arrived cracked and was booked in for next week fits the sentence.\',\\r\\n    why: \\"The only introduces the group that\'s enough. Any phone placed in the same-day repair queue arrived with a cracked screen. Arriving with a cracked screen doesn\'t put a phone in that queue.\\",\\r\\n    readback: \'If a phone is placed in the same-day repair queue, it arrived with a cracked screen.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    sentence: \'Only stalls with a permit sell food at the festival.\', word: \'only\', family: \'necessary\',\\r\\n    target: \'stalls with a permit\', partial: \'stalls\', other: \'sell food at the festival\',\\r\\n    left: \'Stall sells food at festival\', right: \'Stall has permit\',\\r\\n    partialFeedback: \'Stalls alone loses the restriction. [item.right, item.left] : [item.left, item.right];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function MixedItemCheck({ item, index, onChecked, onResult, embedded = false }: { item: MixedItem; index: number; onChecked: (checked: boolean) => void; onResult?: (result: { targetCorrect: boolean; targetIncomplete: boolean; sideCorrect: boolean } | null) => void; embedded?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const [draftDiagram, setDraftDiagram] = useState(\'\');\\r\\n  const [draftReadback, setDraftReadback] = useState(\'\');\\r\\n  const [comparing, setComparing] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const draftQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const readbackQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const draftInput = useRef<HTMLTextAreaElement>(null);\\r\\n  const [target, setTarget] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [left, setLeft] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const targetQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const sideQuestion = useId();\\r\\n  const change = (update: () => void) => { update(); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); onResult?.(null); };\\r\\n  const reset = () => { draftInput.current?.focus(); setComparing(false); setTarget(null); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); onChecked(false); onResult?.(null); };\\r\\n  const targetCorrect = target === item.target;\\r\\n  const sideCorrect = left === item.left;\\r\\n  const number = index + 1;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-lab co-check\\" data-mixed-item={number}>\\r\\n    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote>\\r\\n    <p>Write the complete conditions and the arrow yourself. Then read your diagram back as an if/then sentence. You can use words or short labels you define.</p>\\r\\n    <label htmlFor={draftQuestion}>{embedded ? \'Your diagram\' : `Your diagram for sentence ${number}`}</label>\\r\\n    <textarea ref={draftInput} id={draftQuestion} className=\\"co-attempt-input\\" rows={3} value={draftDiagram} readOnly={comparing} onChange={event => setDraftDiagram(event.target.value)}/>\\r\\n    <label htmlFor={readbackQuestion}>{embedded ? \'Read it in English\' : `Your English readback for sentence ${number}`}</label>\\r\\n    <textarea id={readbackQuestion} className=\\"co-attempt-input\\" rows={3} value={draftReadback} readOnly={comparing} onChange={event => setDraftReadback(event.target.value)}/>\\r\\n    {!comparing && <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={!draftDiagram.trim() || !draftReadback.trim()} onClick={() => setComparing(true)}>{embedded ? \'Compare with the choices\' : `Compare my translation for sentence ${number}`}</button>}\\r\\n    {comparing && <>\\r\\n    <p>{embedded ? \\"Compare your written diagram with these choices. Check that you kept the complete conditions and the arrow\'s direction.\\" : \'Your written answer is saved for you to compare. The choices below check the target and direction; your written answer is not graded automatically.\'}</p>\\r\\n    <h3 id={targetQuestion}>In this sentence, what does <strong>{item.word}</strong> {item.family === \'sufficient\' ? \'make enough\' : \'make required\'}?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={targetQuestion}>{targetOptions(item, index).map(option => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={target === option} onClick={() => change(() => setTarget(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <h3 id={sideQuestion}>Which condition gives you enough to establish the other?</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-labelledby={sideQuestion}>{sideOptions(item, index).map(option => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={left === option} onClick={() => change(() => setLeft(option))}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    {left && <RuleDiagram left={left} right={left === item.left ? item.right : item.left} label={`Your diagram: ${left} implies ${left === item.left ? item.right : item.left}`}/>}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cr-control-row\\"><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"co-primary\\" disabled={target === null || left === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); onChecked(true); onResult?.({ targetCorrect, targetIncomplete: target === item.partial, sideCorrect }); }}>{embedded ? \'Check answer\' : `Check sentence ${number}`}</button><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>{embedded ? \'Revise my answer\' : `Try sentence ${number} again`}</button></div>\\r\\n    <div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`co-feedback ${targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}>\\r\\n      {targetCorrect && sideCorrect ? <>\\r\\n        <strong>Read it back: {item.readback}</strong>\\r\\n        <p>{item.why}</p>\\r\\n      </> : <>\\r\\n      <strong>Revise the part described below.</strong>\\r\\n      <p>{targetCorrect ? `You found the complete target: ${item.target}.` : target === item.partial ? item.partialFeedback : item.otherFeedback}</p>\\r\\n      <p>{sideCorrect ? \'Your arrow keeps the direction the sentence gives.\' : item.reversed}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Here, <strong>{item.word}</strong> points to what is {item.family === \'sufficient\' ? \'enough, so its target goes on the left\' : \'required, so its target goes on the right\'}. {item.why}</p>\\r\\n      <p>Read it back: {item.readback}</p>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n    </div>}</div>\\r\\n    </>}\\r\\n    {!comparing && <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"cr-reset\\" onClick={reset}>{embedded ? \'Revise my answer\' : `Try sentence ${number} again`}</button>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function MixedFamilyPractice() {\\r\\n  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState<boolean[]>(() => mixedFamilyItems.map(() => false));\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <p>These sentences use both kinds of expression. The family name stays hidden until you check your choices. First translate each sentence yourself, without supplied condition labels. Then compare your work and check the complete target and arrow direction before moving on.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\" role=\\"status\\">Sentence {current + 1} of {mixedFamilyItems.length}</p>\\r\\n    {mixedFamilyItems.map((item, index) => <div key={item.sentence} hidden={current !== index}><MixedItemCheck item={item} index={index} onChecked={value => setChecked(previous => previous.map((old, itemIndex) => itemIndex === index ? value : old))}/></div>)}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => setCurrent(current - 1)}>Previous mixed sentence</button><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === mixedFamilyItems.length - 1 || !checked[current]} onClick={() => setCurrent(current + 1)}>Next mixed sentence</button></div>\\r\\n    <p>Moving between sentences keeps your written attempt, choices, and feedback. item: (typeof mixedFamilyItems)[number]; itemIndex: number };\\r\\n\\r\\nconst baselineQuestions: Question[] = [\\r\\n  ...completeConditionItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 0, kind: \'choice\' as const, item })),\\r\\n  ...openingActivityItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 1, kind: \'diagram\' as const, item })),\\r\\n  ...mixedFamilyItems.map((item, itemIndex) => ({ id: `mixed-independent-${itemIndex + 1}`, stage: 2, kind: \'independent\' as const, item, itemIndex })),\\r\\n  ...equivalenceItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 3, kind: \'choice\' as const, item })),\\r\\n  ...transferItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 4, kind: \'choice\' as const, item })),\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nconst errorLabels: Record<MixedDrillError, string> = {\\r\\n  qualification: \'Keeping the complete condition\', direction: \'Reading the arrow in the right direction\',\\r\\n  requirement: \'Separating what is enough from what is required\', equivalence: \'Comparing the whole meaning\',\\r\\n};\\r\\ntype Result = { firstCorrect: boolean; correct: boolean; errors: MixedDrillError[] };\\r\\ntype Check = (correct: boolean | null, errors?: MixedDrillError[]) => void;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ChoiceQuestion({ item, onCheck }: { item: MixedDrillChoiceItem; onCheck: Check }) {\\r\\n  const [picked, setPicked] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"omd-question\\">\\r\\n    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><h3>{item.prompt}</h3>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"omd-options\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Answer choices\\">{item.options.map((option, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={option} aria-pressed={picked === index} onClick={() => { setPicked(index); setChecked(false); onCheck(null); }}>{option}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"omd-primary\\" disabled={picked === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); onCheck(picked === item.correct, picked === item.correct ? [] : [item.errorByOption?.[picked] ?? item.skill]); }}>Check answer</button>\\r\\n    {checked && <div role=\\"status\\" className=\\"omd-feedback\\" data-correct={picked === item.correct}><p>{item.feedback[picked]}</p>\\r\\n      {picked !== item.correct && <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => { setChecked(false); setPicked(null); onCheck(null); }}>Try this question again</button>}\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction DrillQuestion({ question, onCheck }: { question: Question; onCheck: Check }) {\\r\\n  if (question.kind === \'choice\') return <ChoiceQuestion item={question.item} onCheck={onCheck}/>;\\r\\n  if (question.kind === \'independent\') return <MixedItemCheck item={question.item} index={question.itemIndex} embedded onChecked={() => undefined} onResult={result => {\\r\\n    if (!result) return onCheck(null);\\r\\n    const errors: MixedDrillError[] = [];\\r\\n    if (!result.targetCorrect) errors.push(result.targetIncomplete ? \'qualification\' : \'direction\');\\r\\n    if (!result.sideCorrect) errors.push(question.item.family === \'necessary\' ? \'requirement\' : \'direction\');\\r\\n    onCheck(result.targetCorrect && result.sideCorrect, errors);\\r\\n  }}/>;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cir-root omd-diagram\\"><p>Build the original diagram, then read it as an if/then sentence before checking.</p>\\r\\n    <DiagramDrills items={[question.item]} embedded persistProgress={false} allowContrapositive={false} allowNegation={false} shuffleItems={false} explanations={openingActivityExplanations} feedbackForResult={result => {\\r\\n      if (result.correct) return `Your diagram keeps ${question.item.left} as sufficient for ${question.item.right}.`;\\r\\n      const reversed = result.left === question.item.right && result.right === question.item.left;\\r\\n      if (reversed) return `Your arrow makes ${question.item.right} enough to establish ${question.item.left}. [] : [reversed && question.item.group === \'g2\' ? \'requirement\' : \'direction\']);\\r\\n    }}/>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function OpeningMixedDrill() {\\r\\n  const [questions, setQuestions] = useState<Question[]>(baselineQuestions);\\r\\n  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [visited, setVisited] = useState([baselineQuestions[0].id]);\\r\\n  const [results, setResults] = useState<Record<string, Result>>({});\\r\\n  const [finished, setFinished] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [introducedStages, setIntroducedStages] = useState<number[]>([]);\\r\\n  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);\\r\\n  const runner = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);\\r\\n  const question = questions[current];\\r\\n  const stage = stages[question.stage];\\r\\n  const showIntroduction = !finished && !introducedStages.includes(question.stage);\\r\\n  const lastPosition = useRef({ current, finished, showIntroduction });\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (lastPosition.current.current === current && lastPosition.current.finished === finished && lastPosition.current.showIntroduction === showIntroduction) return;\\r\\n    lastPosition.current = { current, finished, showIntroduction };\\r\\n    heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true });\\r\\n    runner.current?.scrollIntoView?.({ block: \'start\', behavior: \'instant\' });\\r\\n  }, [current, finished, showIntroduction]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  function record(id: string, correct: boolean | null, errors: MixedDrillError[] = []) {\\r\\n    setResults(previous => {\\r\\n      const old = previous[id];\\r\\n      if (correct === null) return old ? { ...previous, [id]: { ...old, correct: false } } : previous;\\r\\n      return { ...previous, [id]: old ? { ...old, correct, errors: [...new Set([...old.errors, ...errors])] } : { firstCorrect: correct, correct, errors } };\\r\\n    });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  function move(index: number) {\\r\\n    setCurrent(index);\\r\\n    setVisited(previous => previous.includes(questions[index].id) ? previous : [...previous, questions[index].id]);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  function next() {\\r\\n    if (current + 1 < questions.length) return move(current + 1);\\r\\n    if (questions.length === baselineQuestions.length) {\\r\\n      const errors = [...new Set(baselineQuestions.flatMap(item => results[item.id]?.errors ?? []))];\\r\\n      const confirmations: Question[] = errors.flatMap(error => {\\r\\n        const items = error === \'equivalence\' && results[\'mixed-transfer-4\']?.errors.includes(\'equivalence\') ? [obligationConfirmationItem] : confirmationItemsByError[error].slice(0, 1);\\r\\n        return items.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 5, kind: \'choice\' as const, item }));\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      if (confirmations.length) {\\r\\n        setQuestions(previous => [...previous, ...confirmations]);\\r\\n        setVisited(previous => [...previous, confirmations[0].id]);\\r\\n        setCurrent(current + 1);\\r\\n        return;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    setFinished(true);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const firstCorrect = baselineQuestions.filter(item => results[item.id]?.firstCorrect).length;\\r\\n  const corrected = baselineQuestions.filter(item => results[item.id]?.firstCorrect === false && results[item.id]?.correct).length;\\r\\n  const confirmations = questions.filter(item => item.stage === 5);\\r\\n  return <section ref={runner} className=\\"omd-runner\\" aria-label=\\"Conditional translation practice\\">\\r\\n    <div className=\\"omd-summary\\" hidden={!finished}><h2 ref={finished ? heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>Review your practice</h2>\\r\\n      <p>You answered {firstCorrect} of 24 correctly on your first try. You corrected {corrected} after checking the explanation.</p>\\r\\n      <p>This result covers your answer choices and the diagrams you built with the supplied words. Your written translations are for you to compare with the examples.</p>\\r\\n      {!!confirmations.length && <><h3>How the new sentences went</h3><ul>{confirmations.map(item => <li key={item.id}>{item.id === obligationConfirmationItem.id ? \'What is required and what actually happened\' : item.kind === \'choice\' && errorLabels[item.item.skill]}: {results[item.id]?.firstCorrect ? \'you answered the new question correctly on your first try\' : \'you corrected the new question after checking the explanation. Give this another try with a different sentence\'}.</li>)}</ul></>}\\r\\n      <p>When you return to a sentence, read your diagram back in English. Keep every restriction, and ask whether your arrow promises more than the sentence does.</p>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"omd-primary\\" onClick={() => { setFinished(false); move(0); }}>Review my answers</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"omd-introduction\\" hidden={!showIntroduction}>\\r\\n      <h2 ref={showIntroduction ? heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>{stage.title}</h2>\\r\\n      <p>{stage.purpose}</p>\\r\\n      {stage.example && <blockquote>{stage.example}</blockquote>}\\r\\n      <p>{stage.explanation}</p><p>{stage.instruction}</p>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"omd-primary\\" onClick={() => setIntroducedStages(previous => [...previous, question.stage])}>{question.stage === 0 ? \'Begin practice\' : \'Continue\'}</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <div hidden={finished || showIntroduction}>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"omd-progress-heading\\"><p>{question.stage < 5 ? `Stage ${question.stage + 1} of 5` : \'24 main questions completed\'}</p><p>{question.stage < 5 ? `Question ${current + 1} of 24` : `Fresh check ${current - 23} of ${questions.length - 24}`}</p></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"omd-progress\\" role=\\"progressbar\\" aria-label={question.stage < 5 ? \'Main questions completed\' : \'Fresh checks completed\'} aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={question.stage < 5 ? 24 : questions.length - 24} aria-valuenow={questions.filter(item => (item.stage === 5) === (question.stage === 5) && results[item.id]?.correct).length}><span style={{ width: `${questions.filter(item => (item.stage === 5) === (question.stage === 5) && results[item.id]?.correct).length / (question.stage < 5 ? 24 : questions.length - 24) * 100}%` }}/></div>\\r\\n    <h2 ref={!finished && !showIntroduction ? heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>{stage.title}</h2><p className=\\"omd-purpose\\">{stage.purpose}</p>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    {questions.filter(item => visited.includes(item.id)).map(item => <div key={item.id} hidden={finished || showIntroduction || item.id !== question.id} data-mixed-question={item.id}><DrillQuestion question={item} onCheck={(correct, errors) => record(item.id, correct, errors)}/></div>)}\\r\\n    {!finished && !showIntroduction && <nav className=\\"omd-navigation\\" aria-label=\\"Practice questions\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => move(current - 1)}>Previous question</button><button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"omd-primary\\" disabled={!results[question.id]?.correct} onClick={next}>{current === questions.length - 1 ? (question.stage === 5 ? \'Finish practice\' : \'Review practice\') : \'Next question\'}</button></nav>}\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]
